Planted: Voynich 3/3 recovered (AUC 0.64-0.70), LA 0/3, PE 0/3 (true hidden partition scores z1 = 7-9, but random partitions of 108-283 signs never align with it: max z1 3.3-3.7). (g) Shuffles 0/6 replicated; planted 6/6 recovered (AUC 0.74-1.0); real 60/60 replicate in all three. Replicated classes: Voynich {gallows,d,q,s,x} {l,r,m,n,g} {e,ch,sh,h} {o,a} {y} {i}; LA {logograms} vs {A,I,JA,TA,NA,TE,SA,DA...} vs {KU,KA,*301,SI,RO,ZE...}; PE {final class signs M288,M297,M346,M376...} vs {header signs M157,M327 compounds...} | Machine calibrated (0 FP on 15 shuffles); pure random partitions find planted rules only in small alphabets (Voynich) - for LA/PE search is needed (g: 4/4). All real survivors rediscover known slot structure (Voynich glyph-position classes; LA logogram vs syllabogram slots plus a KU/KA/*301/SI/RO class; PE final-class vs header signs). Held-out replication yes; nothing new beyond the LA two-class syllabogram split (check next). Not cracked. |
| r1:R-1.2 | (b) N=10,000 random value sets per script, 3 total-shuffles + 3 planted value sets; (c) N=5,000 random transforms on the real corpus, 2,000 on each of 2 global shuffles and 2 planted scrambles | (b) PE real: 17 stage-1, 2 replicate on held-out tablets (5/14 add up vs 0.05-0.07 expected); both have N14 = 10, one is exactly N14=10, N45=100, N34=300. Mean tablets balanced at stage 1: 3.1 with N14=10 vs 0.3 otherwise. Shuffles 0/3, planted 3/3 recovered. LA: only 8 sections (3 + 5 after split): 0 survivors, planted 0/3 (underpowered). Voynich zodiac: 32 stage-1, 0/32 replicate; shuffles 0/3; planted 3/3. (c) Written order is never beaten: 0 survivors in Voynich, LA, PE. Non-swap transforms: 0 FP in 6 shuffles, planted 5/6 recovered (dilv, int2, deint2 found). Swap (metathesis) ops are uncalibrated: on Voynich shuffles they create learnable structure (2/2 runs with 60 'replicated' swap survivors) | (b) The machine rediscovers the PE decimal count system (N14=10, N45=100, N34=300) from random guessing alone, with held-out confirmation: a positive control on real data, not new. No zodiac label reads as a running number under any random digit map (0/32 replicate; 3/3 planted numberings found). LA totals too few to test. (c) No reading order beats the written one in any script; swap ops excluded from verdicts. Not cracked. |
| r1:R-1.3 | (d) N=20,000 random (S, class) hypotheses per script, 3 shuffles + 3 planted; (e) all same-shape cross-script pairs (2,392 real), 3 word-shuffles + 3 planted | (d) Real: Voynich 8 stage-1 / 0 replicate, LA 0, PE 0. Shuffles: 0 replicated in 9/9 (stage-1 up to 10 in Voynich: stage 1 is liberal, stage 2 holds). Planted: Voynich 2/3, LA 1/3, PE 1/3 recovered (4/9). (e) Real 0 replicated, shuffles 0/3, planted 0/3: even pairs with 70% identical context only reach matched-null rank 0.02-0.03 (needs < 0.0005) | (d) Inside a Currier language, no glyph n-gram set marks an illustration section on held-out pages; no LA syllable marks a commodity; no PE middle sign marks a number system once the final class sign is removed (power about 1 in 2 for a strong planted rule; absence is an upper bound, not proof). (e) The cross-script pairing machine has no power: 6 context features cannot single out even a planted identical pair, so its null result says nothing. Not cracked. |
| r1:R-1.4 | Site held-out (LA, PE) and transliteration held-out (Voynich) re-test of 53 / 59 / 238 replicated partitions; random-partition control | LA: climbed (g) 40/40 pass off Haghia Triada (median z 10.0, gain 0.19 bits/token); pure random (a) 4/13; random control 11/200 (5.5%). PE: 0/59 pass on the 69 non-Susa tablets (median z 1.4 for g; random control 0/200): underpowered, not a refutation. Voynich IT2a: 238/238 pass (median z 16) vs 78/200 random partitions (Voynich glyph order is so structured that 39% of random partitions pass) | The LA three-class structure (logograms / single-sign 'mark' signs KU, KA, *301, SI, RO, ZE that stand alone on nodules and roundels / ordinary syllabograms) holds at sites outside Haghia Triada: a real, site-independent slot pattern, but known in kind (logogram vs syllabogram slots, single-sign nodule marks). Voynich classes are transcription-robust. PE cannot be tested off Susa (69 tablets). Nothing predicts anything new. Not cracked. |
| r1:R-1.final | ~137,000 random hypotheses on real data (a 30k partitions + 180 climbs, b 30k value maps, c 15k transforms, d 60k sign-class sets, e 2.4k pairs) plus ~670,000 on 42 shuffled and 42 planted control corpora | Survivors replicated on held-out C (real): (a) V 198, LA 13, PE 19; (g) 60/60/60; (b) PE 2 (both N14=10; one = N14 10, N45 100, N34 300), V 0, LA 0; (c) 0/0/0; (d) 0/0/0; (e) 0. Shuffle false positives (runs with >=1 replicated survivor): 0/9 a, 0/6 g, 0/9 b, 0/6 c (non-swap ops; swap ops 2/2 FP on Voynich, excluded), 0/9 d, 0/3 e. Planted recovery: a 3/9 (Voynich 3/3, LA 0/3, PE 0/3), g 6/6, b 6/9 (LA 0/3: 8 sections), c 5/6, d 4/9, e 0/3. Outside re-test: LA classes 40/40 off Haghia Triada; Voynich 238/238 on IT2a; PE 0/59 on 69 non-Susa tablets | Calibrated (0 shuffle FP outside swap ops) and able to find planted rules in a, g, b, c, (d); e has no power. Everything that survives on real data is known structure: Voynich glyph-position classes, LA logogram / single-sign mark / syllabogram slots, PE final-class vs header signs, and the PE decimal count units (N14=10, N45=100, N34=300) found blind from random guesses. Written order is never beaten; no zodiac numbering; no sign marks a commodity or section on held-out data. Grades: PE N14=10 stays B (now independently re-found); LA 'single-sign mark class' C (would support: same class on new nodules; would kill: these signs inside long words at other sites). Not cracked. |
| v15:V-15 | Glyphs as chaff, message in the lengths: word lengths (glyph units, EVA characters, strokes), ',' gaps, words per line, line totals mod k and length differences as the carrier. Structure tests vs word shuffles within line/page/section and a position-preserving null; ~10 M random codebooks + ~16,600 annealing chains over 8 schemes x 7 letter models (la, it, de, cs, he, oc, grc), held-out halves, same search on shuffled nulls. Controls: Caesar planted in the lengths (5 codes), Caesar and Manzoni lengths (loops/v15_*.txt) | No excess repeats (z <= 1.4; planted 82-410); no length link across the line break (z -1.1; planted +56); length no more structured than the first glyph (0.015 vs 0.018). Best Voynich decode +0.03 bits/letter over the best null; planted Caesar +0.3 to +1.3 (readable when lossless); prose lengths +0.1 to +0.4. ',' gaps bunch toward line ends; period-8 rhythm = line layout | Grade A (negative): no hidden letter stream in the lengths, gaps or line counts. Caveat: homophonic keys bend any language model onto a structured stream (planted Latin also 'decodes' as German or Greek), so such searches must be calibrated on natural-language length streams |
| v15:V-15.1.1 | Long repeats in the length stream: distinct length 10-grams seen twice or more, longest repeated length run; z vs within-line / page / section shuffles. Controls: plants, Caesar, Manzoni | Voynich ZL glyph units 17 (null 15.3, z +0.4 / +0.9 / +1.3), EVA chars 12 (z +0.1), strokes 185 (null 164, z +1.4); longest repeat 11-13 = null. IT2a 25 vs 23.9 (z +0.2). Plants: len 1,654 vs 11 (z +410), pair 321 vs 10 (z +82), bacon2 41 vs 29 (z +2.0). Manzoni 24 vs 0.35 (z +39); Caesar 0 vs 0 | NO excess repeats. A message carried by lengths repeats its own words and phrases and leaves repeated length runs (positive controls z 82-410; natural Italian z 39). The Voynich length stream has exactly the repeats its shuffled self has. Only a Baconian-type 5-bit code (z 2) would hide at this test |
| v15:V-15.1.2 | Neighbour MI of lengths inside a line (mi_in), across the line break (mi_x), conditional entropy H(L|2 previous) vs the three shuffles | ZL glyph mi_in 0.021 vs 0.007 line-null (z +26), page +34, section +61; h2 2.715 vs 2.725 (z -8); mi_x 0.016 vs 0.018 (z -1.1). EVA and strokes similar (mi_in z +31 / +41). IT2a z +22; A z +7, B z +24. Plants: len mi_x z +56, pair mi_in z +12, bacon2 z +8; Manzoni mi_in z +120, mi_x z +11; Caesar mi_in z +10 | Lengths of neighbouring words are related inside a line (known), but NOT across the line break (z -1.1, while every planted message and natural Italian join across lines). A hidden length message would run on from line to line; the Voynich length dependence stops at the line edge |
| v15:V-15.1.3 | Is length a privileged partition of the vocabulary? mi_in excess (over within-line shuffle) for the length partition vs 60 random partitions with the same class token masses, and vs a first-glyph and a last-glyph partition (hash mod 10) | Voynich ZL: length 0.0150 (glyph) / 0.0188 (EVA) / 0.0224 (strokes) vs random 0.0007 +- 0.0005 (rank 1/61, x23), BUT first-glyph partition 0.0184 (as large as length); last-glyph 0.0013. IT2a: length 0.0129, first glyph 0.0175. A: 0.0081 vs first glyph 0.0111; B: 0.0170 vs 0.0223. Plants: length 0.003-0.147, first glyph <= 0.0008 (length alone carries it). Caesar: length 0.0054, first 0.0050, last 0.0195; Manzoni 0.0619 / 0.0073 / 0.0173 | KILL for 'glyphs are chaff': when lengths carry the message, the glyph content of words carries nothing (plants: first-glyph excess ~0). In the Voynich the first glyph carries as much order structure as the length, or more, in both transcriptions and both languages. The length sequence is a by-product of word-level structure (the known junction / first-glyph rules), not a separate channel |
| v15:V-15.1.4 | Periodicity: periodogram of the length stream in 120 bands (periods 2-64 words); max ratio to the null mean vs the same statistic in the nulls (family-wise) | ZL glyph max 1.40 at period 8.0 words (null max mean 1.23, max 1.37; p 0.024 = floor with 40 nulls); EVA 1.48 at 8.0; strokes 1.55 at 8.0; IT2a 1.38 at 10.8; A 1.40 at 8.0 (p 0.54), B 1.45 at 8.3 (p 0.10). Plants: no peak near 8 (pair p 0.15, bacon2 p 0.10; len peak at 3.8) | One peak at ~8 words = the mean line length (8.5 words). Lines start with longer words (known, FINDINGS: line-initial words are longer), so the length stream beats at the line period. Layout, not a cipher period. Position-preserving null in cycle 3 |
| v15:V-15.1.5 | Gap channel (ZL3b only): '.' clear space vs ',' uncertain space (8.2% of 30,193 gaps); MI of consecutive gap types inside a line vs gaps shuffled within line (200x) | MI 0.0022 vs 0.0003 (z +11 to +13) | Uncertain spaces cluster: two in a row more often than chance. Could be position in line, cramped lines or transcriber judgement. Tested for position and decoded in cycles 2-3 |
| v15:V-15.1.6 | Words per line: MI of consecutive line word counts inside a paragraph (middle lines) vs line order shuffled within page (200x) | Voynich 0.94 vs 0.47 (z +29 to +32), IT2a z +34, A +18, B +21; plants (same line template) the same z +29-31; Caesar +0.5, Manzoni +3.1 | Neighbouring lines have similar word counts because the writing area changes smoothly down a page (drawings). Layout: the plants, which copy the template, show the same value. Not a channel |
| v15:V-15.1 | Lengths as the message, structure test (6 statistics x 3 units x 2 transcriptions, 3 shuffle nulls, 3 planted messages, 2 natural texts) | No excess long repeats (z <= 1.4; plants z 82-410); no length dependence across the line break (z -1.1; plants z +56); length is no more structured than the first glyph (0.015 vs 0.018; plants 0.003-0.147 vs <= 0.0008); periodicity only at the line period; gap types cluster (z +12) | Grade A (negative): the length channel carries no structure beyond what the words carry, and less than any planted length message. The one unexplained signal, clustering of ',' gaps, goes to cycle 3 |
| v15:V-15.2.1 | POSITIVE controls: Caesar hidden in the word lengths of a Voynich-like text (plant_pair, plant_bacon2, plant_len) | pair scheme: held-out J -3.85 (Latin model) vs null -4.81, margin over the best null +0.93, z +33; sample 'senlaesareetutsupacenpacerisramaniscem' (partly readable Caesar: 'caesar', 'pacem', 'romanis'); a second seed reached J -3.25 with a fully readable decode ('cumcaesareetitsipacempopulusromanus...'). bacon2: J -3.55 vs -4.80, margin +1.20, z +23, 'dactionamcomperetisdiamdicuntqu'. len (lossy, 21 letters on 10 lengths): margin +0.30, z +4.2 (Latin); up to +0.45, z +20 in other languages | The pipeline recovers a length-borne message at +0.9 to +1.3 bits/letter over the best null when the code is lossless and +0.1 to +0.45 when lossy. CAVEAT found: the planted Latin is also 'decoded' by the German, Czech, Greek, Occitan, Italian models with similar margins (+1.1 to +1.3; Hebrew +0.3): a homophonic key with 32-81 symbols can bend any language model onto a language-like stream. The test detects 'a message-like sequence', NOT which language it is in |
| v15:V-15.2.2 | NEGATIVE controls: Caesar and Manzoni's own word lengths (EVA-char channel = letters), same 42 cells each | Caesar: max margin +0.10, max z +6.5, 5/42 cells z > 3; Manzoni: max margin +0.39 (len/Czech), max z +23, 12/42 cells z > 3 | Natural-language length sequences 'decode' weakly above their within-line shuffles because word lengths in real prose alternate (short function words). This sets the bar: any Voynich cell must beat these, not just its own shuffles |
| v15:V-15.2.3 | Voynich ZL3b, 126 cells (glyph units, EVA characters, strokes x 6 schemes x 7 languages) | max margin over the best null: +0.015 (glyph), +0.027 (EVA), +0.020 (strokes); median margin -0.05 to -0.06; max z 3.5 / 1.8 / 4.1, two cells z > 3 of 126 (baconT/Greek glyph z 3.5, baconT/Italian strokes z 4.1), both BELOW their global-shuffle null (margins -0.04, -0.05); no decode readable (samples 'euosienuaituteeeeuseire...') | NO decoding. The Voynich length channels decode worse than the planted messages by ~1 bit/letter and worse than plain Latin or Italian prose lengths (Manzoni z 23). Two z > 3 cells out of 126 is the expected tail and both fail the second null. Survivors: none |
| v15:V-15.2.4 | Gap channel (ZL ',' uncertain space vs '.' as Baconian bits, 7 languages, 8 within-line gap shuffles) and words-per-line channel (12 symbols, 7 languages; nulls: line order within page / global) | gaps: max margin +0.012, max z +2.4 (Italian), samples 'loiioilniiiiiitdlisii...'; words per line: max z +0.5, every cell below the global null (margin -0.27 to -0.45) | NO decoding in the gaps or in the line counts |
| v15:V-15.2 | Lengths as the message, massive random codebook search (~5 M random keys, ~7,800 annealing chains, 7 language models, 6 schemes, 5 channels, held-out halves, shuffled-length nulls, planted and natural controls) | Planted Latin recovered at +0.9 to +1.3 bits/letter (readable Caesar); natural prose lengths reach +0.1 to +0.4; Voynich best +0.03, nothing beats both nulls at z > 3 | Grade A (negative, within the tested schemes): no length, stroke, gap or line-count code of these six kinds carries a Latin, Italian, German, Czech, Hebrew, Occitan or Greek letter stream. Upper bound: a lossless message filling >= half the words would show at ~1 bit/letter; a lossy one-letter-per-length code at ~0.3; the Voynich margin is 0.03 |
| v15:V-15.3.1 | Period-8 peak of cycle 1 under the position-preserving null | ratio at period 8: glyph 1.35, EVA 1.40; null max mean 1.22-1.25, max 1.32-1.36; p 0.032 (floor with 30 nulls); highest real bands now also at periods 25-64 | The line-period beat survives a null that keeps each word's index from the line start. It is not the line-initial effect alone; what remains is consistent with line-END effects (index from the end is not kept) and slow drift down the page, both layout. Not tested further. Grade C: layout rhythm, not a cipher period |
| v15:V-15.3.2 | Neighbour length MI inside a line under the position-preserving null | glyph 0.0214 vs 0.0051 (z +31); EVA 0.0253 vs 0.0048 (z +31) | The neighbour dependence of lengths is not positional. It is the known word-to-word junction coupling (V-15.1.3: the first glyph carries as much) |
| v15:V-15.3.3 | Uncertain-space (',') clustering: position profile, position-preserving null, page dispersion | ',' rate rises along the line: 0.087 at gap 0, 0.062-0.072 at gaps 1-4, 0.095 at 7, 0.12-0.17 at gaps 9-12. MI of consecutive gap types 0.0022 vs 0.0002 under the position null (z +15). Page dispersion chi2 272 on 206 df (p ~ 0.001; top pages f14v 13/63, f56v 13/74, f36v 11/60) | Uncertain spaces bunch together, more toward line ends and on some pages: cramped writing as the line runs out of room, plus page-to-page differences in spacing or transcription. Cycle 2 found no code in them (max margin +0.012). Grade B: a spacing/crowding effect |
| v15:V-15.3.4 | One letter per line: line glyph totals mod k (k 20-30) and words per line, 7 languages | POSITIVE: plant_total margin +0.73 to +1.12 over the best null, z +17 to +38 in all 7 languages. NEGATIVE: Caesar max margin +0.027 (z 2.0), Manzoni +0.004 (z 1.5). Voynich glyph totals: max margin +0.030, max z +3.0 (Latin, k 30), 0/7 cells z > 3; words per line max z +1.2, margin -0.014 | NO decoding. Line totals carry no letter stream; a planted one is found at ~1 bit/letter even with only ~2,000 training lines |
| v15:V-15.3.5 | Delta channel (L_t - L_t-1) mod 6 / 8 / 10, 7 languages | POSITIVE: plant_delta margin +0.26 (mod 6, lossy), +0.52 (mod 8), +0.77 (mod 10), z 12-57. NEGATIVE: Caesar max margin -0.001, Manzoni +0.14 (mod 10, z 7.2). Voynich: max margin +0.018 (mod 6), max z +2.8, 0/21 cells z > 3 | NO decoding in length differences |
| v15:V-15.3 | Stranger channels (line totals mod k, length differences) and the two cycle-1 leftovers under position-preserving nulls | Planted messages found at +0.26 to +1.12 bits/letter; Voynich best +0.030; ',' gaps cluster toward line ends (crowding); the period-8 rhythm is line layout | Grade A (negative) for line-total and delta codes; Grade B for ',' clustering as crowding; Grade C for the period-8 rhythm as line-end layout |
| v15:V-15.final | Word lengths (glyph units, EVA characters, pen strokes), uncertain-space gaps, words per line, line totals and length differences as the carrier of a hidden letter stream. (1) Structure: repeats, neighbour MI across and within lines, entropy rate, periodicity, 'is length a privileged vocabulary partition', against word shuffles within line / page / section and a position-preserving null. (2) Search: ~6,700 solver runs, ~10 M random codebooks scored, ~16,600 annealing chains; 7 letter models (Latin, Italian, German, Old Czech, Hebrew, Occitan, Greek); 8 schemes (len, pair, bacon2, baconT, tri3, mod4pair, line total mod 20-30, delta mod 6-10); held-out halves; the same search on shuffled-length nulls. (3) Controls: Caesar planted in the lengths (5 codes) and natural Latin and Italian lengths | (1) No excess structure: no excess repeated length runs (z <= 1.4; planted z 82-410), no length link across the line break (z -1.1; planted z +56), and length is no more structured than the first glyph (0.015 vs 0.018; in planted texts the first glyph carries ~0). (2) Best Voynich decode +0.03 bits/letter over the best null, never readable; planted Caesar +0.3 to +1.3 (readable when the code is lossless); natural prose lengths +0.1 to +0.4. (3) Side results: uncertain spaces bunch toward line ends (crowding); a period-8 rhythm in lengths is line layout | Grade A (negative): the Voynich length channels carry no hidden letter stream of these kinds, in any of the seven languages, at a size where a planted one is recovered with a 10-40x margin. Upper bound: a lossless code in >= half the words would give ~1 bit/letter, a lossy one-letter-per-length code ~0.3; observed 0.03. Lengths behave as a by-product of the words (the junction coupling), not as a separate channel. Methodological caveat (new): a homophonic key bends any language model onto a structured stream, so the planted Latin also 'decodes' as German, Czech or Greek at nearly the same margin. Such searches detect 'message-like', not 'which language', and must be calibrated on natural-language length streams, which themselves beat their shuffles at z up to 23. Not tested: codes using visual gap widths (needs image measurement; ZL ',' marks only uncertain spaces), lengths read in another order, codes spanning < 1/4 of the words |
| v16:V-16 | THE SPACES ARE LIES: delete the spaces, re-insert them by 80,000 random mechanical rules (13 families: before/after glyph sets, glyph pairs, every k, shift, jitter, keep/add subsets; held-out lines) and by free simulated annealing, scored by MDL (adaptive token code + bigram-spelled lexicon), Zipf and junction MI. Nulls: within-line glyph shuffle, Markov-2 resynthesis. Negative: true-spaced Latin/Italian. Positive: Latin/Italian re-spaced by a planted rule (loops/v16_*.txt) | Rules: Voynich best -0.086 / -0.081 b/g (ZL merges, IT splits), Markov-2 null -0.124, Latin -0.019 (the -que clitic), Italian 0, planted -0.19 to -0.66. Annealing: Voynich -0.17 / -0.14 (F vs written 0.87), Latin -0.15 (0.91), Markov-2 -0.17 (0.86), planted -0.32 to -0.45 (0.60-0.81). Written vs no spaces at all: Latin 2.93 / 3.58, Voynich 2.72 / 2.65. Space residual entropy given +-2 glyphs: Voynich 0.19-0.22, Markov-2 0.22, Latin/Italian 0.37; run length adds nothing. Junction excess after re-spacing 0.19 -> 0.13 | No re-spacing beats the written one beyond the Markov null; no counting or glyph-set rule survives. The spaces sit where the glyph stream has its seams, are more predictable than real word breaks, and the words they mark save nothing over a glyph-bigram code (Grade B). Junction effect and line-initial chain survive re-spacing |
| v16:V-16.1.0 | Calibration: MDL2 bits/glyph on test lines for written spacing, NO spacing (each line one unit = pure glyph-bigram coding) and every glyph | Latin written 2.929 / none 3.584 / every1 3.996; Italian 3.153 / 3.565 / 4.015; Voynich ZL 2.720 / 2.650 / 3.905; IT2a 2.685 / 2.645 / 3.890; Markov-2 null 2.785 / 2.663 / 3.908 | In Latin and Italian the written words save 0.41-0.65 bits per glyph over a word-free glyph-bigram description. In the Voynich the written words cost 0.04-0.07 bits per glyph MORE than no spacing at all, like the Markov-2 resynthesis (0.12). The Voynich 'words' do not pay for their lexicon. Grade B (two transcriptions, size-matched controls) |
| v16:V-16.1.1 | Negative control: true-spaced Latin (Caesar) and Italian (Manzoni), 8,000 rules each | Latin: 11 of 8,000 rules (0.14%) beat the written spacing on train; the best on test is 'written + break before q' (-que clitic split) at -0.019 b/g. Italian: 0 of 8,000 (best +0.000) | PASS. Real word breaks are near-optimal; the only improvement found is a real morpheme boundary |
| v16:V-16.1.2 | Positive control: Latin and Italian with spaces deleted and re-inserted by a planted rule (every 5 letters; break vowel->consonant, min unit 3) | Rules beating the planted spacing 16-47%; best test gain -0.19 to -0.66 b/g; boundary F against the TRUE words rises from 0.17 / 0.13 / 0.20 / 0.36 (planted) to 0.37 / 0.40 / 0.50 / 0.38 (best rule) | PASS (direction). The search always rejects a planted spacing and moves toward the real words, though a single glyph-set rule cannot spell out real words (F <= 0.5) |
| v16:V-16.1.3 | Voynich ZL3b, 8,000 rules | 14.5% of rules beat the written spacing on train. Best on test: after{y,n,h} (break after y, n, h; written spaces elsewhere ignored), 2.635 vs 2.720 (-0.086 b/g, -3.2%). Units 12,054 vs 17,500 tokens; types 5,415 vs 4,475; length 6.5 +- 4.1 vs 4.5 +- 1.6; Zipf -1.02 / R2 0.968 (written -1.03 / 0.974); boundary F vs written 0.70; junction excess 0.146 vs 0.186. Next best: keep_after{C,y,n,m,...} (delete written spaces not preceded by these) | The best rules MERGE words: they keep spaces after y/n/m (word-final glyphs) and drop most others |
| v16:V-16.1.4 | Voynich IT2a, same | 10.5% beat. Best on test: written + break after {y,s,l,r,n,F,x}, 2.604 vs 2.685 (-0.081). Units 21,704 (shorter, 3.6 glyphs), types 2,709; Zipf -1.21; boundary F 0.87; junction excess 0.253 vs 0.171 | Here the best rule SPLITS words. ZL and IT agree on the size of the gain but not on its direction (merge vs split): no single re-spacing rule is transcription-robust |
| v16:V-16.1.5 | Null: ZL glyphs shuffled within each line (written space positions kept) | 94% of rules beat the written spacing; best -0.956 b/g (every 2 glyphs / near single glyphs) | Sanity: when the spaces truly are unrelated to the glyphs, almost every rule beats them by ~1 bit/glyph. The Voynich is nowhere near this |
| v16:V-16.1.6 | Null: order-2 glyph+space Markov resynthesis of ZL (its spaces come from a local rule) | 18% beat; best -0.124 b/g (after{m}: whole lines as units; Zipf collapses, slope -0.12) | Search-size correction: a text whose spaces are generated locally is beaten by MORE (-0.124) than the Voynich (-0.086 / -0.081), with more winning rules (18% vs 14.5% / 10.5%). The Voynich gain is not beyond this null |
| v16:V-16.1.V | Verdict, cycle 1 | Voynich sits between true-spaced language (gain 0.00-0.02, <=0.14% of rules) and a locally generated spacing (0.12, 18%), nearer the latter; planted spacings are beaten by 0.19-0.66 | NO re-spacing rule beats the written Voynich spacing beyond the Markov-2 null, and the best rule flips between merge (ZL) and split (IT). The visible spaces are not a simple mechanical lie hiding better units. But they also do not behave like Latin word breaks: no spacing at all compresses as well (V-16.1.0). Grade B: 'Voynich written words do not pay for their lexicon under MDL'. Grade C, failed: 'a glyph-set rule recovers hidden units' |
| v16:V-16.2.1 | Negative control: anneal from the written spacing of true-spaced Latin and Italian | Latin: MDL2 2.713 -> 2.564 (-0.148 b/g), boundary F vs written 0.911, 22,215 units vs 19,667 words (endings split off). Italian: 2.997 -> 2.839 (-0.159), F 0.899 | Free MDL search 'improves' real word breaks by ~0.15 b/g by splitting endings; it keeps ~90% of them. This is the calibrated size of a meaningless improvement |
| v16:V-16.2.2 | Positive control: anneal from a planted spacing | Planted every-5 / VC spacing beaten by 0.33-0.45 b/g (2-3x the true-spaced gain); boundary F vs planted 0.60-0.81; F vs TRUE words rises 0.17 -> 0.38, 0.13 -> 0.24 (Latin), 0.20 -> 0.40, 0.36 -> 0.47 (Italian) | PASS in direction and size: planted spacings are abandoned twice as hard and the search moves toward the real words. Recovery is partial (F <= 0.47): a low-temperature search cannot fully escape a bad start |
| v16:V-16.2.3 | Voynich ZL3b and IT2a, anneal from written | ZL 2.679 -> 2.511 (-0.169), F vs written 0.871; IT 2.648 -> 2.507 (-0.141), F 0.878. Units 34,925 vs 35,069 tokens (same count), types 4,180 vs 7,205, length 4.5 +- 1.9 vs 4.5 +- 1.6, Zipf -0.93 (R2 0.979) vs -1.04 | Voynich gain and boundary agreement sit inside the true-language band (-0.15/-0.16, F 0.90-0.91) and far from the planted band (-0.32/-0.45, F 0.60-0.81). By this test the written Voynich spaces behave like real boundaries, not like a planted mechanical spacing |
| v16:V-16.2.4 | Null: Markov-2 resynthesis (spaces from a local chain) and within-line glyph shuffle | Markov-2: -0.173, F 0.860 (same as Voynich). Glyph shuffle: -0.728, F 0.236 | The test cannot tell a locally generated spacing from real word breaks (Markov-2 lands with the languages). It CAN tell a spacing unrelated to the glyphs (shuffle) and a planted mechanical spacing. So the Voynich spaces are 'glyph-coherent', which a Markov-type generator also gives |
| v16:V-16.2.5 | Search from a random spacing (density 0.22) | Latin ends at +0.539 b/g above its written words (F 0.51); Voynich +0.058 (F 0.46); Markov-2 -0.018 (F 0.47) | Power limit: the annealer does not rediscover words from scratch even in Latin. Side result consistent with V-16.1.0: an arbitrary local optimum is almost as good as the written spacing in the Voynich (+0.06) but far worse in Latin (+0.54) |
| v16:V-16.2.6 | Junction effect after free re-spacing (excess MI last glyph -> next first glyph, within-line unit shuffle) | ZL 0.188 -> 0.129 (-31%); IT 0.175 -> 0.124 (-29%); Markov-2 0.202 -> 0.127 (-37%); Italian 0.079 -> 0.054; Latin 0.038 -> 0.048; planted every-5 0.54 -> 0.27 | The junction effect SURVIVES re-spacing: about 70% remains, still 2.5-3x the languages. It is not an artefact of where the scribe put the spaces. It shrinks by the same fraction as in the Markov-2 null |
| v16:V-16.2.V | Verdict, cycle 2 | - | No: the best free re-spacing of the Voynich improves on the written spaces only by the amount real word breaks are improved (and by the amount a Markov chain's spaces are improved); planted mechanical spacings are improved 2-3x more. The spaces are not lies of the planted kind. Grade B (two transcriptions, both controls behave). Caveat: a Markov-2 null passes too, so this does not show the spaces are LINGUISTIC word breaks, only that they sit where the glyph stream itself has its seams |
| v16:V-16.3.1 | Controls for space predictability | Planted every-5: 1.01 from glyphs, 0.00 with run length (rule found exactly). Planted VC (+min-length filter): 0.34 -> 0.19. Glyph shuffle: 0.79-1.08 (unpredictable). True Latin 0.33 (k=3) / 0.33 with run length, accuracy 0.95; Italian 0.34 / 0.32 | PASS: the test finds a counting rule exactly and a glyph rule largely; real word breaks keep ~1/3 of their entropy given 3 letters each side |
| v16:V-16.3.2 | Voynich ZL / IT2a | ZL residual 0.266 (k=1), 0.216 (k=2), 0.219 (k=3); with run length 0.231 / 0.220 / 0.225; accuracy 0.959. IT2a 0.256 / 0.194 / 0.195; run length 0.204 / 0.190 / 0.192; accuracy 0.96 | Voynich spaces are MORE predictable from the glyphs than Latin or Italian word breaks (0.19-0.22 vs 0.32-0.34 of their entropy left), but not determined: ~4% of gaps are mispredicted, and the run length adds nothing (0.216 -> 0.220). The 'fixed glyph count' hypothesis is killed (would give ~0, as the planted every-5 does). Grade B |
| v16:V-16.3.3 | Null: Markov-2 resynthesis | 0.275 / 0.223 / 0.233; run length 0.245 / 0.237 / 0.245; accuracy 0.958 | Identical to the Voynich. The extra predictability of Voynich spaces is what any text with Voynich-like glyph statistics and locally conditioned spaces shows; it needs no hidden spacing rule beyond 'spaces follow the same local statistics as glyphs' |
| v16:V-16.3.4 | What the annealed units are (cycle-2 masks, written start) | Voynich ZL: 71% of units are whole written words, 19% sub-word pieces, 10% straddle a written space (IT2a 72 / 19 / 9; Markov-2 68 / 22 / 9; Latin 75 / 23 / 1.5; Italian 76 / 20 / 4). Reuse (tokens of types seen twice+) 0.95 vs 0.86 written; hapax types 43% vs 69%. Voynich edits: 13% of written spaces deleted (Latin 2.3%), as many breaks added; commonest deletions r|a (627), l|C, s|a, l|k, r|o; additions l|o, l|C, l|d, o|d, r|o. Markov-2 null: the same list in nearly the same order. Top units: daiin, Cedy, y, ol, Sedy, qokeedy, qokeey | Straddling units are 2-7x more common in the Voynich than in the languages, and the Voynich edits are a SWAP (delete and add equally) around word-edge glyphs l, r, s, o, whereas Latin only splits endings. But the Markov-2 null makes the same swaps at the same glyph pairs. The units 'hidden across the spaces' are the ones glyph statistics alone would propose. Grade B as description; the 'real units straddle the spaces' reading: C, failed against the Markov null |
| v16:V-16.3.5 | Positional behaviour and line-initial chain under re-spacing | MI(unit among top 30, line-initial): written 0.017 -> annealed 0.016 (IT 0.017 -> 0.016); line-final 0.013 -> 0.012. Chain first unit of line r -> line r+1: written +0.016 (z 3.7), annealed +0.016 (z 4.1), random-start anneal +0.002 (z 0.7); first-glyph chain 0.128 (z 34-36) in every segmentation (it is invariant: line-initial glyphs do not move). Latin chain unit -0.011, glyph +0.016 (z 2); Markov-2 glyph +0.007 (z 2) | The line-initial chain survives any re-spacing (it lives in the first glyph, which no spacing changes) and the Markov-2 null does not produce it (z 2). Line-position vocabulary effects are unchanged by re-spacing. Both line effects are independent of where the spaces are |
| v16:V-16.3.V | Verdict, cycle 3 | - | The written spaces are not a mechanical rule of the counting or glyph-set kind: they are more predictable than real word breaks, exactly as predictable as the spaces of a Markov-2 chain, and the residual ~20% of their information is not explained by run length. Nothing distinguishes them from 'spaces placed by the same local statistics as the glyphs'. Junction effect (V-16.2.6) and line-initial chain (V-16.3.5) both survive re-spacing |
| v16:V-16.F1 | Does any re-spacing beat the written Voynich spacing beyond the null? | Rule search: best held-out gain -0.086 (ZL) / -0.081 (IT) bits/glyph with 10-15% of rules beating written; Markov-2 null -0.124 (18%); Latin -0.019 (0.14%, the -que clitic), Italian 0; planted -0.19 to -0.66. Free annealing: Voynich -0.169 / -0.141 (F vs written 0.87-0.88); Latin -0.148 (0.91), Italian -0.159 (0.90), Markov-2 -0.173 (0.86), planted -0.32 to -0.45 (0.60-0.81) | NO. Every Voynich gain is inside the Markov-2 null and near the true-language band, and the best rule flips between merging (ZL) and splitting (IT). Grade B |
| v16:V-16.F2 | What rule? | Best ZL rule after{y,n,h}; best IT rule 'written + after{y,s,l,r,n}'. Counting rules (every k, shift d) are the WORST families on the Voynich (+0.38-0.42 b/g). Run length adds nothing to space prediction (0.216 -> 0.220), while a planted counting rule is found exactly (1.0 -> 0.0) | No mechanical rule survives: 'after a fixed glyph count' killed; 'break before/after a glyph set' not transcription-robust and not beyond null. Grade C, demoted (failed) |
| v16:V-16.F3 | What the units look like | Annealed Voynich units: same token count (34,925 vs 35,069), 42% fewer types (4,180), length 4.5 +- 1.9, Zipf -0.93; 71% whole words, 19% sub-word, 10% straddle a space (Latin 1.5%, Italian 4%, Markov-2 9%). Edits swap boundaries at r|a, l|C, s|a, l|k (deleted) and l|o, l|C, l|d, o|d (added); the Markov-2 null makes the same edits | Straddling units exist, but they are what glyph statistics alone propose. Grade B description |
| v16:V-16.F4 | New side result: Voynich words do not pay for their lexicon | MDL2 on held-out lines, written / no spaces at all: Latin 2.93 / 3.58, Italian 3.15 / 3.57, Voynich ZL 2.72 / 2.65, IT 2.69 / 2.65, Markov-2 2.79 / 2.66. Space predictability (residual entropy, k=2): Voynich 0.22 / 0.19, Markov-2 0.22, Latin 0.37, Italian 0.37 | In Latin and Italian, the written words save 0.4-0.65 bits/glyph over a word-free glyph-bigram code; in the Voynich they cost 0.04-0.07 MORE, like a Markov chain's 'words'. Voynich spaces are better predicted by the glyphs than real word breaks. Grade B (two transcriptions; one Latin and one Italian control; bigram spelling model). Would kill: a higher-order spelling model under which Voynich words save as much as Latin words |
| v16:V-16.F5 | Do the junction effect and the line-initial chain survive? | Junction excess after free re-spacing: ZL 0.188 -> 0.129, IT 0.175 -> 0.124 (~70% kept; Latin 0.038 -> 0.048, Italian 0.079 -> 0.054); Markov-2 0.202 -> 0.127. Line-initial first-glyph chain 0.128 (z 34-36) under every spacing (Markov-2 z 2); first-unit chain z 3.7 -> 4.1 | Both survive. The junction effect is not an artefact of where the spaces were drawn (Grade A that it survives re-spacing; its cause is still open, and a Markov-2 chain reproduces its size). The line-initial chain is independent of spacing by construction |
| v16:V-16.F | Overall | - | THE SPACES ARE NOT LIES of a mechanical kind. Hidden units straddling the spaces: not found beyond the null. The written spaces sit where the glyph stream has its seams (like a Markov chain's spaces), are more predictable than Latin or Italian word breaks, and the 'words' they mark carry no compression beyond glyph bigrams. Not cracked; nothing predicted outside the corpus |
| v12:V-1.1 | Lag profile: context = the word 1, 2, 3, 4 places back. A k-way weave puts the link at lag k, a single text at lag 1. Positives: verbose Latin + verbose Markov filler alternating (LF-alt), Latin + Italian alternating (LI-alt), Voynich Currier-A stream + Currier-B stream alternating (VV-alt). Negatives: verbose Latin, Italian, Dante, plain Latin, line-shuffled Voynich | lag1 / lag2 excess: **ZL 0.303 / 0.049, IT 0.284 / 0.052**; LF-alt 0.181 / 1.194, LI-alt 0.061 / 0.235, VV-alt 0.085 / 0.316; vLatin 0.102 / 0.032, vItalian 0.286 / 0.025, vDante 0.364 / 0.039, pLatin 0.180 / 0.063; Shuf-line -0.013 / 0.008. Ratio lag2/lag1: positives 2.8-6.6, negatives 0.09-0.35, **Voynich 0.16-0.18** | Every planted alternating weave is found (lag 2 beats lag 1 by 3-7x). Voynich has the profile of a single text: the link sits at lag 1 and drops by 6x at lag 2, like Italian and Dante. No odd/even weave |
| v12:V-2.1 | Fixed k-way streams with separate tables per stream: parity (k=2), mod 3, mod 4. Same controls | par2 / mod3 / mod4: **ZL 0.066 / 0.065 / 0.102; IT 0.072 / 0.079 / 0.097**; LF-alt 1.131 / 0.156 / 0.366; LI-alt 0.212; VV-alt 0.291; vLatin 0.049 / 0.013 / 0.001; vDante 0.033 / -0.008 / 0.001 | No k = 2, 3 or 4 stream in Voynich is more regular than the woven line (all < 1/3 of lag-1 0.30). Small Voynich excess for per-position tables (mod4 0.10 vs lag4 0.03) is a position effect (line-initial and early words differ, known), not a stream: it does not appear at lag 4 itself |
| v12:V-3.1 | Streams continued over line breaks (a woven continuous text keeps each stream across the break): parity by paragraph-global token index, contexts carried; also lag 1 carried | par2_carry: **ZL 0.010, IT 0.023**; LF-alt 1.156, LI-alt 0.220, VV-alt 0.298; vLatin 0.030, Shuf-line 0.009 | Planted streams survive the break; Voynich parity streams carry nothing across (shuffle level). Whatever resets at the line break (FINDINGS V3) is not two continuous texts |
| v12:V-4.1 | Alternation by glyph class: stream = class of first glyph / last glyph / gallows, bipartition searched greedily on the training fold (objective: same-stream junction MI minus shuffle), scored on the test fold | Searches on Voynich end on degenerate splits (one side = rare glyphs F, m, n, z / b, c, u; gallows 't' vs 'F'); held-out excess 0.26-0.29 = the lag-1 value, i.e. no gain from splitting. Power is limited: on LF-alt the same search only reaches 0.12-0.14 (verbose symbols are shared by both streams), so a class weave whose classes do not follow single glyphs could be missed | Null for glyph-class weaves (with the power caveat). The latent-switch model in cycle 2 removes the need to guess the class |
| v12:V-5.1 | Regularity side-metrics: repeated word-bigram tokens and Hamming-1 neighbours per 1000 pairs at lag 1 and lag 2, excess over shuffle | Repeated bigrams lag1 / lag2: **ZL 50 / 12, IT 50 / 14**; LF-alt -22 / 198; VV-alt 459 / 424 (duplicated stream); vLatin 100 / 49. Hamming-1: ZL 2.6 / 2.6 (both lags), positives and negatives within +-1 except VV-alt | Voynich phrases repeat at lag 1, not at lag 2, as in prose. The Hamming-1 excess (the tandem swaps) is equal at lags 1 and 2: neighbour similarity reaches two words, which is NOT the weave pattern (weave: lag 2 only). Looked at in cycle 3 |
| v12:V-1.2 | W2 vs H2 held-out likelihood. Positives: LF-alt (strict alternation, Latin + filler), LF-hmm (Markov switch P = 0.6, stay 0.4), LI-alt, VV-alt. Negatives: vLatin, vItalian, vDante, pLatin, Shuf-line, Gloss-pair | W2 - H2 (bits/token, mean of 2 folds): **Voynich ZL -0.271 (folds -0.40, -0.15), IT -0.315 (-0.34, -0.29)**; LF-alt +0.983 (learned P(stay) 0.00-0.07 = alternation recovered), LF-hmm +0.174 (+0.29, +0.06; learned stay 0.42-0.49 vs planted 0.40); LI-alt -0.074, VV-alt -0.178 (EM failed: stay 0.39-0.60, no alternation found); vLatin -0.015, vItalian -0.171, vDante -0.205, pLatin -0.119, Shuf-line -0.054, Gloss-pair -0.180 | The latent weave model finds both Latin+filler weaves blind, including the random-switch one that fixed schemes missed, and recovers the switch rate. It does NOT find weaves of two look-alike texts from random starts (repaired in cycle 4). **Voynich gives the most negative W2 - H2 of all 12 corpora**: after any switch it is much better to condition on the word just before than on the last word of 'the same stream'. No latent two-stream weave |
| v12:V-2.2 | Side result: what the 2-state HMM (H2) itself buys over one stream (H2 - k1) | Voynich 0.72 / 0.71 bits; prose 0.58-1.06; Shuf-line 0.71 | A 2-state mode switch helps every text by a similar amount, including line-shuffled Voynich, so it captures vocabulary modes (section, Currier language, position), not order. Voynich 'modes' persist (P(stay) 0.60-0.73, highest of all corpora with Dante and Gloss-pair): runs of similar words, not alternation |
| v12:V-1.3 | All glyph pairs that alternate between neighbouring words: Hamming-1 neighbours (same length, one glyph differs) by pair, at lag 1 and lag 2, vs shuffle | ZL lag 1: 690 vs 593 (z 4.6), lag 2: 579 vs 509 (z 4.3); IT 643/562 (4.0), 562/496 (4.4). Lag-1 swaps: l/r 93 vs 69 (z 3.6), k/t 69/49 (3.5), l/o 32/13 (6.5), ch/cth 24/13 (4.0), ch/k 19/12; IT the same (l/r z 6.8, k/t 2.7, ch/cth 3.6). a/o is NOT enriched as a one-glyph swap (34 vs 32). **ch/sh is enriched only at lag 2** (ZL 63 vs 43, z 6.6 / 3.5 at 50 shuffles; IT 72 vs 46, z 4.5 / 4.8) and not at lag 1 (44 vs 50; 48 vs 52) | The tandem swaps are a handful of specific glyph pairs (l/r, k/t, l/o, ch/cth). Total swap excess is the same at lags 1 and 2 (~+100 and ~+70), so 'neighbour similarity' reaches two words, which a two-stream weave would not do (weave: lag 2 only). One pair, ch/sh, shows the weave shape (A _ A' but not A A') |
| v12:V-2.3 | ch/sh lag-2 pattern against the position-preserving null; where in the line; what sits in the middle | Survives: ZL 63 vs 35.0 (z 4.4), IT 72 vs 35.3 (z 6.0); lag 1 null (z 0.4, 1.0), lag 3 null (37 vs 37). Concentrated at words 2 and 4 of the line (ZL 20 vs 9.1, IT 24 vs 9.3 by the positional null), rest spread. Pairs: shedy _ chedy (14-18), chedy _ shedy (9), shey _ chey, chol _ shol; the middle word is usually a q-/o- word (qokaiin, qokar, otol, qotedy) and rarely itself a swap of either end (5-6 of 63-72) | A real but SMALL effect (~30 extra pairs in 30,000 lag-2 pairs), transcription-robust, not explained by position vocabulary. Shape: 'sh-X q-Y ch-X' (the same word with sh then ch, one word apart, mostly words 2-4). It is a local echo, not a stream: it does not repeat at lag 4 or 6 and no other glyph pair does it. Grade C lead: a sh/ch alternation rule around an intervening q-word |
| v12:V-3.3 | Phase / pairing: excess gallows concordance, Hamming-1 rate and a/o concordance at each junction j (word j -> j+1), odd-minus-even contrast. Gloss-pair (text + gloss pairs) is the positive control | Voynich gallows concordance per junction: -48, +15, +43, +52, +48, +11, +27, +27 (per mille), odd-even z -0.7 (ZL), -0.6 (IT); Hamming-1 z 0.1 / 0.9; a/o z 3.4 / 3.2 but carried by junction 1 alone (+44, then +3, +16, +2, 0, -10). Gloss-pair gallows odd-even z 2.8 (weak: pairs are not aligned to line starts after pouring); weaves and prose |z| <= 2.1 | No pairing phase in Voynich lines: concordance is a flat neighbour effect from junction 2 on, with a line-start anomaly at junction 1 (the first word differs, known). Words are not grouped into fixed text+gloss pairs |
| v12:V-4.3 | Second-order alternation I(x_t; x_t-2 given x_t-1) for first glyph, gallows, a/o, last glyph, excess over shuffle (millibits) | Voynich ZL: 1.1 / 2.6 / 2.2 / -0.1 (IT 6.3 / 2.9 / 2.8 / -0.2); positive weaves: LF-alt 240 / 10 / - / 149, LF-hmm 40 / 0.7 / - / 18, LI-alt 16 / 5 / - / 78, VV-alt 93 / 23 / 2 / 31; single texts: vLatin 15 / 0.8 / - / 17, vItalian 0.4 / 1.3 / - / 4, vDante 11 / 0.5 / - / 5, pLatin 23 / 0.5 / 0.2 / 34; Shuf-line ~0 | Voynich carries less second-order (A B A) information than any non-shuffled control, single prose included. The lag-2 information it has is already explained by lag 1 (a first-order chain), the opposite of a weave |
| v12:V-5.3 | Filler: is one stream low-entropy? (a) word-type entropy at odd vs even line positions, equal samples, first word removed; per-position entropy 1-9. (b) W2 weave model fitted on the whole corpus, decoded: per-stream type entropy, top words, P(switch) at swap junctions. Positive control LF-hmm (Latin + 40-word Markov filler, random switch) | (a) ZL positions 3,5,7.. 9.818 vs 2,4,6.. 9.743 bits (diff +0.075, null -0.032, z 2.9; IT z 3.3). Per position ZL 9.12, 8.65, 8.70, 8.74, 8.79, 8.76, 8.67, 8.60, 8.56 (smooth: position 2 lowest, a trend not a zig-zag; IT shows a faint zig-zag 8.65 / 8.81 / 8.66 / 8.79). (b) LF-hmm: decoded streams agree 89% with the planted truth, filler stream entropy 5.6 vs text 10.0 bits. **Voynich ZL streams 9.58 vs 9.82 bits, IT 9.56 vs 10.09**, shares 47/53 and 45/55, P(stay) 0.64-0.71; the 'streams' are vocabulary modes (daiin, dar, chey, dy vs qokeedy, qokeey, qokedy, chol, ol) and swap their words between ZL and IT (ol, chol change side). P(switch) at Hamming-1 junctions 0.19 / 0.15 vs 0.35 / 0.32 elsewhere (Shuf-line 0.09 vs 0.48: similar words are put in one stream by any model) | No filler stream: the planted filler is recovered at 89% and sits 4.4 bits below the text; Voynich's two decoded 'streams' differ by 0.2-0.5 bits, persist in runs and look like the known Currier A-like (daiin, dar) vs B-like (qok-edy) vocabularies. Swaps do NOT mark switches (switch probability is lower, not higher, at a swap). The small odd/even entropy difference is a position trend (word 2 is the least varied), not a filler slot |
| v12:V-1.4 | W2 vs H2 again with structured starts. Positives LI-alt, VV-alt (look-alike streams, missed in cycle 2), LF-hmm; negatives vLatin, vItalian | W2 - H2 (fold 1, fold 0): LI-alt -0.076, -0.054; VV-alt -0.009, -0.111 (the parity start never wins on training likelihood); LF-hmm +0.216, +0.130; vLatin +0.029, -0.072; vItalian -0.068, -0.120; **Voynich ZL -0.202, -0.136; IT -0.188, -0.176** | Power limit confirmed, not repaired: a latent switch cannot pull apart two streams that look alike (verbose Latin vs verbose Italian, Currier A vs B). Those weaves are caught only by the fixed lag test of cycle 1 (lag 2 >> lag 1), which Voynich fails. For streams that differ (text + filler), the latent model wins by +0.13 to +0.22 bits; Voynich sits at -0.14 to -0.20, below both single-text controls |
| v12:V-2.4 | 3-way weave: W3 vs H3 (states = stream + last index of every stream), Voynich ZL | W3 - H3: -0.277 (fold 1), -0.264 (fold 0); W3 P(stay) 0.43-0.47; H3 beats H2 by 0.40-0.46 bits (more vocabulary modes) | No 3-way weave: conditioning on the last word of the same 'stream' is worse than on the immediately preceding word by 0.27 bits, more than at k = 2. Caveat: no planted 3-stream control finished; the fixed mod-3 test (cycle 1, V-2.1) is the controlled 3-way check and was also null |
| v12:V-12.F | (1) Fixed weaves: lag-k link bits, parity / mod-3 / mod-4 streams, streams carried over line breaks, glyph-class streams searched on a training fold. (2) Latent weave W-k (context = last word of the SAME hidden stream) vs an HMM H-k with identical parameters (context = previous word), k = 2 and 3, EM with random, parity and glyph-class starts, held-out by folio parity. (3) Tandem swaps: Hamming-1 neighbours by glyph pair at lags 1-3, phase by junction, second-order (A B A) information, decoded-stream entropy, P(switch) at swaps, position-preserving null. Controls: 4 planted weaves (verbose Latin + verbose filler, alternating and random switch; Latin + Italian; Voynich A-stream + B-stream), 4 single texts (verbose Latin, Italian, Dante; plain Latin), text+gloss pairs, line-shuffled Voynich | Planted weaves found: lag2/lag1 2.8-6.6 (single texts 0.09-0.35); W2 - H2 +0.98 (alternating filler), +0.13 to +0.29 (random switch, streams decoded 89% right, filler stream 5.6 vs 10.0 bits). **Voynich: lag2/lag1 0.16-0.18; parity streams over line breaks 0.01-0.02 bits (shuffle level); class splits give no gain; W2 - H2 -0.14 to -0.40 and W3 - H3 -0.26 to -0.28, the most negative of every corpus; second-order A B A information 0-6 millibits (lowest of all non-shuffled texts); decoded 'streams' differ by only 0.2-0.5 bits and are the Currier-like modes (daiin, dar vs qokeedy, qokedy)** | **Voynich lines are NOT woven from separable streams** under any scheme tried: odd/even, every 3rd or 4th word, by glyph class, by a hidden 2- or 3-state switch, continuous across lines or reset per line. Each word is best predicted by the word right before it, more so than in prose. There is no filler stream and no text+gloss pairing. Power limit: two weaved streams that look alike are invisible to the latent model, but they are caught by the lag test (lag 2 >> lag 1), which Voynich fails |
| v12:V-12.S | Tandem swaps characterised | Lag-1 swap pairs above chance: l/r (93 vs 69), k/t (69 vs 49), l/o (32 vs 13), ch/cth (24 vs 13), ch/k; same in IT. a/o is not a one-glyph swap (34 vs 32). Swaps sit at lag 1 and lag 2 alike, do not raise the switch probability (0.15-0.19 at a swap vs 0.32-0.35 elsewhere), and show no odd/even phase. **One pair has the weave shape: ch/sh is enriched only at a one-word gap** (shedy _ chedy, chey _ shey, chol _ shol; ZL 63 vs 35 and IT 72 vs 35 under a position-preserving null, z 4.4 / 6.0; adjacent 44 vs 42; gap of 2 words 37 vs 37), mostly words 2-4 of a line, with a q-/o- word in the middle | The swaps are neighbour assimilation inside one stream, not stream boundaries. New grade-C lead: an 'sh-X ... ch-X' echo across one intervening (usually q-) word. Small (~30 extra pairs in ~30,000), transcription-robust. Would support: the same echo in held-out hands/sections and a preference for the order sh -> ch; would kill: disappearance within one Currier language or hand |

| v20:V-20.F5 | Weak lead | op- initial words are the least over-dispersed word class across pages in both transcriptions (R 1.14 / 1.17), ranked like a planted soft quota, but within the detector's noise | Grade C (would support / kill in V-20.5.5) |
| v20:V-20.F | Overall | - | THE PAGES WERE NOT WRITTEN TO A QUOTA. No glyph, pair, word class or linear combination is conserved per page, paragraph or bifolio; the one conserved line quantity is a product of word order (bigram chain reproduces it). A filler or exercise written to a per-page target is ruled out at the strength any planted target was found at |
| r2:R-2.final | ~8.9 million programs on 21 corpus x mode runs: 3 real scripts, 13 nulls, 3 planted generators (grille, 2 ledgers), 2 natural languages (Latin, Linear B); layout-given rerun on 11 | Planted generators recovered 3/3, the mechanism readable in the winning program (`PL 0 LI if tab1` = grille row from the line number; `... AB ... tab2` = commodity cycle read from the line above): +24 to +97 mbit/token on held-out text. Linear B: no gain (0/30). Latin: gain only from line-end layout; with layout given <= 2 mbit/token. Real, layout given, vs KN + document cache: LA +28 bits net (p 0.14), PE +20 (p 0.28), Voynich -50 on KN alone (Markov resynthesis +42). Shuffles always beat KN (+97 to +3,900 bits) via the token skeleton | No program beats the baselines on held-out data beyond its nulls in any script. What the search finds in LA and PE is 'copy what the line or entry above had' (logograms, entry-initial signs, word length): within-document repetition, which a cache model already explains (known in kind: list entries repeat). Voynich glyph order is not reproduced by any short procedure better than a 3-glyph Markov chain; Markov-resynthesised text gains slightly more from the search than the real text. Grades: 'no short generator (<= 15 ops, these primitives) for Voynich' A (negative, calibrated: planted grille found at 24.7 mbit/token, real <= 0); 'LA/PE entries copy from the line above' B (known kind); procedural origin of any of the three texts: not supported. Not cracked |

| r2:R-2.1b | Planted ledger, PE-sized (same generator on PE signs), 150k + 150k | Winner `WI P2 AB WI mod 6 min AW2 if tab2` (reads AB = commodity in the line above, i.e. 'next in the cycle'): net C +1,714 bits (96 mbit/token), 30/30 significant; line start +1,612, symbols M038/M153/M124 = the planted cycle | PASS |
| r2:R-2.1c | Planted table-and-grille text, Voynich-sized (12-row table of Voynich-like words; word in line l, slot w = row (l+w) mod 12; 20% random real words). 50k + 50k | Winner `PL 0 LI if tab1` = 'at line start look up the line number': net C +1,834 bits (24 mbit/token), 30/30 significant; all gain at line start. The (l+w) row step inside the line is NOT found (KN-3 already follows the word chain) | PASS (partial): the procedural part that Markov cannot see (line number -> grille row) is recovered |

AW2 here = the sign in the same slot two lines up (PE has no word spaces): a copy-from-above rule. Would kill: z < 1 on new pages, or an equal effect in a known-language manuscript with short repetitive words |
