Word choice, length, junction and repeat structure flow across ink darkenings as they do in Latin; a generator restarting at each dip is excluded (planted version found 3/3). Left over: one weak tendency (C) and one small same-load similarity (C+), both needing better dip detection. Not cracked; nothing predicted outside the corpus |


### v24: the scribe's corrections are the rulebook (4 Oct 2026; `loops/v24_final.txt`)

| id | method and control | result | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| v24:V-24.1.0 | Census of Voynich corrections in transcription markup (ZL3b, IT2a, GC2a, RF1b, VT0e, CD2a, FG2a, LSI 0d) | ZL is the only file that marks visible changes: 20 <!corr?>, 1 <!erased?>, 1 'k on top of e', 1 's above e', 1 's above last a' = 24 marked sites on 22 lines. Before-state reconstructable for 4 (chek over chee; dchsed with s added above; ko[o:l] and [s:]qokeodaiin, both states given, direction unknown). LSI has 16 'blot' notes and one "'ai' overlapped". The 817 ZL [a:b] alternatives are reading ambiguities (o/a 161, s/r 118, y/o 50 ...), not marked corrections | The markup corpus is tiny: 24 corrections, 4 with a before-state. Upper bound for marked corrections: about 1 per 1,600 words |
| v24:V-24.1.1 | POSITIVE generator control: planted 4-slot template + random junction table (36,000 words); 5% random substitutions, a slip is corrected (p 0.9) only if it breaks the template parse or the junction table, else (p 0.03); 1,267 corrections | Corrections enforce legality: big1 z +13.4, bilp +12.6, slot +4.8, junction +3.6, line-initial +3.0 (all p 0.0005, 154 rules FW-significant). Conditional legal|identity: big1 z +9.2, junction +4.2. Before-forms are words 0.15 vs null 0.27 | Recognised as rule repair. But identity|legality is ALSO +8.2: the coarse legality proxies (bigrams, v7 slot order) do not capture the generator's exact parse, so word identity absorbs the rest. The identity-vs-legality split is only as good as the legality proxy |
| v24:V-24.1.2 | POSITIVE language control (planted): Caesar, 5% substitutions, non-words corrected p 0.85, real-word slips p 0.15; 861 corrections | identity|legal z +4.5; but legal|identity z +21 (junction +24, first glyph +33). Before-forms words 0.01 vs null 0.05 | FAILED as built: injected glyphs were uniform over the alphabet (incl. rare letters) while the null draws by frequency, so the null mismatch alone made 'legality' light up. Rebuilt in cycle 2 with frequency-weighted slips |
| v24:V-24.1.3 | REAL Latin control: SCTA/LombardPress diplomatic transcriptions of Petrus Plaoul (Reims, Sorbonne, St Victor, Vatican + 1 witness; 1.04 M words); TEI <del>/<add>/<subst> -> before/after text, word-aligned; 2,342 word-level changes (1,511 whole-word deletions, 398 insertions, 433 replacements); 153 single-letter edits (11 sub, 100 del, 42 ins) | Before-forms are REAL WORDS 0.35 vs null 0.12 (vocab z -11.4, logf z -12.4, FW p 0.04); bigram-legal 0.99 vs 0.98 (z -1.4); bilp z -5.8. All 247 multi-letter replacements: before is a word 0.69 | Real Latin corrections are NOT 'non-word -> word' repairs beyond the null: the scribe's errors were legal, often real words (dicunt/ducunt, destruet/destruat, alius/aliud), fixed for sense or grammar. The textbook expectation (language = misspelling repair) is wrong for real copying; the real-language signature is 'legal before, often another real word' |
| v24:V-24.1.4 | Voynich ZL-marked corrections with a before-state (n = 4) | Before-forms are words 3/4 vs null 0.25; bigram-legal 4/4 vs 0.77; vocab z -2.7, bilp -2.4, first-glyph -2.6; FW p 0.58 (zmax 3.9) | Points the Latin way (legal before, often a real word), not the generator way, but n = 4 with 2 of unknown direction: no family-wise result. Grade C |
| v24:V-24.1.V | Verdict, cycle 1 | - | The method separates a planted rule-repairing generator (before-forms illegal and rarer words than random edits) from real Latin (before-forms legal and more often real words than random edits). The Voynich has only 4 reconstructable corrections in all transcriptions; they lean Latin-like, which is not significant. Next: transcriber-ambiguity control, a planted COPYING control (generator copied from an exemplar with neighbour-glyph slips), rebuilt language control, power at n = 4 |
| v24:V-24.2.1 | Generator control rebuilt (frequency-weighted slips, rule-breaking slips corrected), n 500 | Before is a word 0.19 vs null 0.28 (z -6.6; ratio 0.68). identity|LP z -0.9 (p 0.30); legality|identity: junction z +4.9, big1 -2.2 | PASS: with a trigram-LP match the identity leak of cycle 1 vanishes, and the generator shows pure junction/legality repair |
| v24:V-24.2.2 | Language control rebuilt (Caesar, frequency-weighted slips, non-words corrected), n 500 | Before is a word 0.03 vs 0.05 (ratio 0.6). identity|LP z +2.5 (p 0.009); legality|identity big1 +0.5, q3lp -0.8, junction +0.8 | PASS: pure word-identity repair is recognised (identity beyond legality, no legality beyond identity) |
| v24:V-24.2.3 | NEW COPYING control: the generator text copied from an exemplar; slips take the glyph at the same position of the next/previous word (p 0.7) else a random glyph; every slip restored (p 0.9) whatever rule it breaks; n 500 | Before is a word 0.40 vs 0.33 (z +4.2; ratio 1.2); bigram-legal 0.79 vs 0.75; D for vocab -4.1, q3lp -5.0, first glyph -5.5; identity|LP -0.7; legality|identity big1 -1.2, q3lp -2.4 | A meaningless generator COPIED by a scribe gives 'before-forms more word-like than random', the same sign as real Latin. Only the size differs (1.2x vs 2.5x). So the sign of this signature alone cannot separate meaning from mechanism once copying is possible |
| v24:V-24.2.4 | Real Latin (Plaoul), end-of-word a/b/c sigla insertions removed, n 143 | Before is a word 0.30 vs 0.12 (z +8.9; ratio 2.5); legal 0.99 vs 0.98; identity|LP z -3.4; legality|identity q3lp -5.7 | Real copying errors are legal, word-like slips that the scribe fixes for sense; neither 'identity' nor 'legality' repair beyond the null. Latin and the copying control share the sign; Latin is stronger |
| v24:V-24.2.5 | Voynich ZL-marked corrections (n 4) | Before is a word 3/4 vs 0.25 (Poisson-binomial tail p 0.027; ratio 3.0); legal 4/4 vs 0.77. Share of random 4-subsets with >= 3 word befores: generator 0.02, Latin 0.08, copying 0.19, ZL [a:b] ambiguity pairs 0.45 | Nominally Latin- or copy-like, but n = 4, 2 of the 4 have unknown direction, and ordinary reading-ambiguity pairs produce the same pattern 45% of the time. Not evidence. Grade C |
| v24:V-24.2.6 | TRANSCRIBER-AMBIGUITY control: 435 ZL [a:b] single-glyph alternatives (o/a, s/r, y/o ...), second reading as 'before' | Second reading is a word 0.58 vs 0.23 (z +20.9; ratio 2.5); legal 0.98 vs 0.88; every D z strongly negative (q3lp -24, vocab -21); FW-significant rules are rare glyphs (aj, jy, final g) | Reading ambiguities between similar shapes give pairs where both readings are words: the 'Latin' signature at full strength with no correction involved. Any Voynich 'correction' set taken from transcriber variants is contaminated by this |
| v24:V-24.2.V | Verdict, cycle 2 | - | Discriminator works on the planted pair (generator: legality|identity +4.9, identity|LP -0.9; language: identity|LP +2.5, legality|identity ~0). But real Latin, a copied generator and transcriber ambiguity all share the 'before is a real word' sign, so the question 'meaning or mechanism' turns into 'how much more word-like than a copied generator', which needs many genuine corrections. Voynich markup gives 4 |
| v24:V-24.3.1 | Detector: each word's ink columns split into one segment per glyph unit; segment darkness = mean of its darkest 30% stroke pixels; residual on glyph type x page; anomaly = residual - word median, / page MAD. 11,584 words, 54,346 glyphs; flag = top 2% (z >= 5.2), 232 words | - | - |
| v24:V-24.3.2 | PLANTED control: 1,142 twin words with one glyph double-inked (c' = 1-(1-c)^2 on its stroke pixels) | Flagged 87%; flagged glyph = planted glyph 98% (chance 23%); mean z at the planted glyph 8.3 | The detector sees a strong double-inking and localises it |
| v24:V-24.3.3 | VALIDATION on real marks: the 5 ZL-noted corrections on these pages (kaich f107v, cheam f108r, ykair and chcphy (erased?) f112r, f58v.3) | Anomaly quantiles 0.78, 0.26, 0.81, 0.55, 0.89; none in the flagged top 2% | KILLED as a correction detector: real noted corrections do not look like the planted double-inking |
| v24:V-24.3.4 | Transcriber disagreement at glyph level (ZL [a:b], 143 glyphs; ZL vs IT single-glyph differences, 228 glyphs) vs other glyphs of the same type, label permuted within glyph type x page | [a:b]: +0.06 z vs null -0.01 +- 0.13 (p 0.28); ZL/IT: -0.12 vs -0.01 +- 0.10 (p 0.86) | Disputed glyphs are not darker. Word level: flagged words carry a disagreement 6.5% vs 3.6% +- 1.2% (page x length stratified, p 0.017): dark blots make words harder to read, not necessarily corrected |
| v24:V-24.3.5 | Rule-position test at flagged glyphs (231) vs random glyph of the same word and vs anomaly glyph of unflagged words of the same length; 10 core rules | Flags sit at word edges: last glyph 98 (uniform 47), first 70 (47), middle 63 (137). Last-glyph constraint z +8.4 (within) / +7.6 (between), junction +3.5 within; FW p < 0.001 | Explained by where the pen lands and lifts (ink pools at stroke starts and ends), which also puts the flags on the glyphs that junction and last-glyph rules constrain. With the detector failing validation (V-24.3.3) this is a pen effect, not evidence of rule repair |
| v24:V-24.3.V | Verdict, cycle 3 | - | The images do not yield a correction set: local darkness anomalies are pen-landing effects at word edges, miss all 5 transcriber-noted corrections, and are not enriched at disputed glyphs. The Voynich correction corpus stays at 24 marked sites (4 with a before-state) |
| v24:V-24.4.1 | Word ratio (before-forms that are words / random-edit expectation) per control, all sites | Generator 0.58 (n 1,254); copied generator 1.19 (1,639); real Latin 2.45 (143); ZL [a:b] ambiguity 2.53 (435) | Three regimes: rule repair < 1 < copying < Latin |
| v24:V-24.4.2 | Power: bootstrap samples of size n from each control; share of Latin-like samples above the 95th percentile of copy-like samples (and copy vs generator) | Latin vs copy: n 4 0.45, 8 0.63, 16 0.82, 24 0.91, 32 0.95, 48 0.99. Copy vs generator: n 16 0.75, 24 0.88, 32 0.96. Ambiguity vs copy: n 16 0.95 | About 30-50 genuine corrections with a known before-state are needed to tell Latin-like repair from a copied generator at 95% power; the Voynich markup has 4. Prediction for anyone with the manuscript in hand: collect >= 50 before/after pairs from erasures and overwrites (not from transcriber variants, which look Latin-like by construction) |
| v24:V-24.4.3 | After-only test: the 20 ZL-noted corrections whose after-state is readable; is the corrected word one that occurs elsewhere? Null: random tokens of the same glyph length (4,000 draws); Latin reference: corrected words in Plaoul | Voynich 10/20 = 0.50 vs 0.79 (p 0.001); hapax-like corrected words: cholohaiin, tolchdaiin, okaos, pdaiiiy, sheshe, kaich, dchsed. Latin: 0.90 vs 0.99 for random tokens (n 390) | In both scripts corrections land on rarer words than average (Latin 10x hapax enrichment, Voynich 2.4x). Two caveats prevent a reading: the Voynich null is length-matched and the Latin one is not, and a transcriber writes 'corr?' more readily on an odd-looking word. Grade C: would support 'mechanism' if, in a correction set drawn from the images, corrected Voynich words stay far rarer than Latin's at the same corpus size; would kill if the gap closes |
| v24:V-24.4.V | Verdict, cycle 4 | - | The method needs ~50 genuine Voynich corrections and has 4. No rule is enforced beyond the null in the Voynich data; the planted and Latin controls show the test would see one |
| v24:V-24.F1 | How many Voynich corrections are recorded? | All 8 public transliteration files: only ZL marks visible changes: 24 sites (20 corr?, 1 erased?, 'k on top of e', 's above e', 's above last a'), 4 with a recoverable before-state (2 of unknown direction). The 817 ZL [a:b] variants are reading ambiguities. Image route (25 pages, local ink anomaly): planted double-inking found 87% / localised 98%, but the 5 noted corrections on those pages are not flagged (quantiles 0.26-0.89) and disputed glyphs are not darker (p 0.28, 0.86); flags sit at word edges where the pen lands and lifts | 24 corrections, 4 usable. Upper bound for marked corrections ~1 per 1,600 words |
| v24:V-24.F2 | Does the test work? (controls) | Planted generator with rule-breaking slips corrected: legality|identity junction z +4.9, identity|LP -0.9, word ratio 0.58. Planted language with non-words corrected: identity|LP z +2.5, legality|identity ~0. Real Latin (143 single-letter corrections): word ratio 2.45 (before-forms legal and often real words: dicunt/ducunt, alius/aliud), neither identity nor legality repair beyond the null. NEW copied-generator control (exemplar restored after neighbour-glyph slips): word ratio 1.19, same sign as Latin | Planted rule repair vs word repair: separated. But real copying (Latin) and a copied meaningless generator share the 'before is a real word' sign; only the size differs |
| v24:V-24.F3 | What do the Voynich corrections enforce? | n 4: no rule beyond the null family-wise (zmax 3.9, FW p 0.58). Before-forms 3/4 words (random edits 0.25, tail p 0.027), 4/4 legal: Latin- or copy-like, but ordinary ZL ambiguity pairs give >= 3/4 word pairs in 45% of 4-subsets | Nothing established. Grade C lean toward 'copy-or-language', not toward 'illegal sequence repaired' |
| v24:V-24.F4 | Power | Latin-like vs copy-like at 95% needs ~32-48 genuine before/after pairs; copy vs rule-repair ~32 | Prediction: >= 50 erasure/overwrite pairs read from the vellum would decide it; transcriber variants cannot (they look Latin-like by construction: word ratio 2.53) |
| v24:V-24.F5 | After-only lead | 20 ZL-noted corrected words: only 10/20 occur elsewhere vs 0.79 for same-length tokens (p 0.001); Latin corrected words also rarer than average (0.90 vs 0.99) | Grade C: corrections fall on rare words in both scripts; transcriber selection is the simplest cause |
| v24:V-24.F | Overall | - | THE CORRECTIONS ARE TOO FEW TO BE A RULEBOOK. The method separates planted rule repair from planted word repair, and shows that real Latin corrections are neither (legal slips into other real words). A copied meaningless generator shares that sign, so meaning vs mechanism needs ~50 genuine Voynich corrections; transcriptions give 4, and ink anomalies on 25 pages do not find the known ones. Negative/inconclusive. Grade A for the census (24 marked, 4 usable); C for every Voynich lean |

### v21: forge the Voynich, then catch the forgery (4 Oct 2026; `loops/v21_final.txt`)
Twelve forgers (junction resyntheses, slot generator, page topic, kitchen sink, line-width filling, a GRU language model, copy-and-vary, survivor-targeted) against ~52,000 page discriminators (ridge on random 6-feature subsets, full ridge, boosting) and CNN line/paragraph models, cross-validated by page. Controls: forgery vs forgery, forger output re-forged by itself, a planted long-range re-use, and Latin and Italian herbals forged the same way.

| Run | Method and control | Result | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| v21:V-21.1 | Forger ladder F0-F7 vs 79 page features, 5 seeds; null, negative, planted, Latin/Italian herbal controls | Every forger caught: F3 junction resynthesis 0.933, F7 kitchen sink + width 0.957 (null 0.50, negative 0.59, planted 0.70; Latin 0.92, Italian 0.92) | No forger passes. Herbals are caught by topic re-use, the Voynich by local self-similarity and in-page drift |
| v21:V-21.2 | GRU forger (F8) and CNN line/paragraph discriminators | GRU caught at 0.997 (invents non-words). CNN: F3 0.54-0.59, null 0.50, but planted long-range re-use missed (0.50) | Learned local models are blind beyond ~9 units; not used for verdicts |
| v21:V-21.3 | Copy-and-vary forgers F9/F10 (rich junction key, Timm-Schinner self-citation with real edit operations, paragraph topic), ZL3b and IT2a | F10 0.934 / 0.928 (negative floor 0.66); herbals 0.90/0.84 (floor 0.64). Voynich survivors: para_first_in_rest -6.8, vert_last +6.2, mg_line_var -5.4, first_eq_lag1 +5.2 | Margin over floor equals the herbals', on a different axis: layout-local, never topical |
| v21:V-21.4 | F11 adds one mechanism per survivor; fresh seeds | Targeted survivors closed, AUC unchanged (0.926); near_far (+8 to +9) and ch/sh runs (-4.6) surface; herbals still caught by topic (0.93/0.90). vert_last is not vertical (same at column offsets, z 6.4 vs page line-shuffle) | Whack-a-mole. Generator-like in kind, beyond every single known generator. Grade B |
| v21:V-21.1.1 | Ladder on Voynich: F0 section x position unigram; F1 whole-book junction (v17 rule); F2 F1 + position tables; F3 junction per section + position (v17 resynthesis); F4 slot generator (in-word unit trigram per section x position class); F5 F3 + page topic (lam 0.3: draw from the page's own successor table); F6 F5 + line-initial chain + reduplication + self-citation, (lam, cite, redup) grid-calibrated to the real repeat-by-distance profile (V: 0.35, 0.01, 0); F7 F6 filling each line to the real line's width instead of its word count | Page AUC (ridge / boosting, mean of 5 seeds): F0 0.946/0.955; F1 0.999/0.998; F2 0.999/0.999; F3 0.933/0.950; F4 0.998/0.993; F5 0.952/0.940; F6 0.952/0.946; F7 0.957/0.945. Random 6-feature subsets beating the null's 99th percentile (0.597): 78-98% | Every forger is caught at AUC >= 0.93. Section tables matter most (F1/F2 -> F3: 0.999 -> 0.933); the slot generator is the worst forger (0.998). Page topic, chain, reduplication, self-citation and line-width filling do not bring the Voynich closer (0.95) |
| v21:V-21.1.2 | NULL: forgery vs forgery (two seeds of the same forger as 'real' and 'forged'), F3 and F7, all corpora | V F7 0.500/0.485, V F3 0.486/0.522, LA 0.530/0.507, IT 0.484/0.502; subset AUC max 0.58-0.63, 0-4% above null 99th | Clean: the discriminators find nothing in forgery vs forgery |
| v21:V-21.1.3 | NEGATIVE: F7 output treated as real, re-forged by F7 refitted on it | V 0.590/0.577, LA 0.540/0.560, IT 0.541/0.521; only feature at |z|>=4 in every seed: n_tok (V; width filling loses ~3% of tokens), rep_rare -4.5 | Near-clean. A residual 0.54-0.59 comes from F7's token-count drift and from vocabulary shrinkage on resampling; it sets the floor any 'surviving' signal must clear |
| v21:V-21.1.4 | POSITIVE: Latin and Italian herbals through the same ladder (F1, F3, F5, F6, F7; calibrated the same way: LA lam 0.2, IT cite 0.01) | LA: F1 1.000, F3 0.988, F5 0.974, F6 0.986, F7 0.924; IT: F1 1.000, F3 0.993, F5 0.985, F6 0.987, F7 0.923. Surviving features vs F7 (all 5 seeds |z|>=4, controls |z|<3.5): LA para_first_in_rest +8.1, ttr -7.3, rep_any +7.3, rep_line2_4 +7.1, rep_adj -6.7, near_far +6.2, rep_prevline +5.5; IT wlen_ac2 +9.2, rep_any +8.5, ttr -8.5, rep_sameline +7.4, rep_line2_4 +7.3, rep_adj -6.3 | Passes: real herbals are caught through meaning-level features (topic words named in a paragraph's first line and re-used 2-4 lines later, low TTR, no adjacent repeats) |
| v21:V-21.1.5 | PLANTED: F7 Voynich forgery with a long-range dependency planted (a word of each paragraph's first line re-used once in 35% of later lines), re-forged by F7 refitted on it | AUC 0.702/0.695 (neg floor 0.59); caught by rep_prevline +5.4, para_first_in_rest +4.6, rep_line2_4 +3.5 | Found, at a weak plant (~1 token in 25) |
| v21:V-21.1.6 | VOYNICH vs best forger: features surviving all 5 seeds at |z|>=4 with null and negative |z|<3.5 | vs F7: wlen_ac1 +7.8, para_first_in_rest -7.0, near_far +7.0, first_eq_lag2 +6.6, rep_sameline +6.3, last_eq_lag2 +6.1, near_prev +6.0, glyph_cos_halves -5.1, rare5 -5.1, logfreq_mean +4.9. vs F3: line_n_vs_mwl -11.1, chain_same -6.9 also (absorbed by F7's width filling and chain) | The Voynich is caught as hard as the real herbals (0.957 vs 0.92), but by DIFFERENT features: local self-similarity (repeats inside a line, near-repeats 1+ lines apart, first and last glyphs echoing two words later, positive word-length autocorrelation) and drift inside the page. Its paragraph-first line is LESS re-used later (z -7), where the herbals re-use it MORE (+8). Grade B: the Voynich has structure beyond every forger here, of a copy-and-vary kind, not of a topic kind |
| v21:V-21.2.1 | F8 neural forger vs the cycle-1 feature battery (3 seeds, ridge / boosting / 900 random 6-feature subsets) | ridge 0.997-0.999, boosting 0.988-0.993. Caught by logfreq_sd z -31.6, corpus_hapax -17.2 (it invents non-words), line_glen_sd -13.7, line_n_vs_mwl -12.2, mg_line_var -10.2 | The small GRU is a WORSE forger than the junction rule: it writes plausible glyph strings but too many unseen words and no line-width or line-end discipline. Not the best rung |
| v21:V-21.2.2 | CNN line / paragraph discriminators vs F3, F7, F8 (seed 0; F7 also seed 1) | Line AUC (page AUC): F3 0.544 (0.594); F7 0.620/0.660 (0.716/0.854); F8 0.648 (0.709). Paragraph: F3 0.583 (0.589); F7 0.778/0.794 (0.857/0.877); F8 0.862 (0.921) | The learned model sees F7's line-filling artefact: F7 picks the line-final word by length, so its last words are 1.0 unit too long (5.40 vs 4.36) and m/g-final too rarely (12.5% vs 17.4%). Against the plain junction resynthesis F3 the CNN is near chance (0.54-0.59): locally the junction rule is a good forgery |
| v21:V-21.2.3 | CNN controls: null (F7 vs F7, 2 seeds), negative (F7 output re-forged), planted long-range repeat, positive herbals vs F7 | Null 0.484-0.512; negative 0.483-0.506; PLANTED 0.502/0.516 (NOT found); Latin 0.501 line / 0.568 para (0.614 page); Italian 0.562 / 0.749 (0.809) | Calibrated but blind to long range: the CNN misses the planted re-use that the feature battery finds (0.70), and it barely separates real Latin from its forgery. Its near-chance score on F3 therefore says nothing about structure longer than ~9 units. Learned local models do not beat hand-built page features here |
| v21:V-21.3.1 | F9 and F10 vs Voynich ZL3b and IT2a | ZL: F9 0.941/0.927, F10 0.934/0.938 (ridge/boosting); IT2a: F7 0.952/0.939, F10 0.928/0.928 | Copy-and-vary barely moves the score (0.957 -> 0.934). Transcription-robust |
| v21:V-21.3.2 | Controls for F10: null; negative (F10 output re-forged by refitted F10); planted long-range repeat | Null 0.482/0.499; NEGATIVE 0.662 (V), 0.637 (LA), 0.637 (IT): self-citation is not idempotent (re-forging copies of copies), so the floor rises; only feature failing the negative control in every seed: corpus_hapax / logfreq / rare5 (vocabulary shrinkage). Planted 0.763/0.737 (floor 0.66) | Calibrated; any claim must clear 0.66, not 0.5 |
| v21:V-21.3.3 | POSITIVE herbals vs F10 | Latin 0.897/0.878 (floor 0.637), Italian 0.844/0.850 (floor 0.637). Survivors: LA para_first_in_rest +7.6, ttr -7.5, rep_any +7.5, page_hapax -7.4, rep_otherpara +6.7, rep_line2_4 +6.6, jacc_paras +6.1, rep_rare +5.7; IT rep_adj -6.5, last_eq_lag2 +6.1 | Passes: real text stays caught by topic re-use across lines and paragraphs, which no local copy-and-vary rule supplies |
| v21:V-21.3.4 | VOYNICH survivors vs F10 (all 5 seeds |z|>=4; null and negative |z|<3.5), both transcriptions | ZL: para_first_in_rest -6.8, vert_last +6.2, mg_line_var -5.4, first_eq_lag1 +5.2 (near_far +8.1 fails the negative bound at 3.6). IT2a: near_far +9.3, para_first_in_rest -7.4, vert_last +6.7, first_eq_lag1 +6.6, mg_line_var -5.8. Margin over floor: V 0.27, LA 0.26, IT 0.21 | The Voynich is as far from the best generator as real herbals are, but on a different axis. What survives is LAYOUT-LOCAL, not topical: (1) paragraph-first lines whose words are NOT re-used below (herbals: re-used, +7.6); (2) a word's last glyph echoing the word directly above it; (3) about one m/g per line, more evenly than any forger; (4) neighbouring words sharing their FIRST glyph (alliteration, beyond the last->first junction). No topic re-use survives. Grade B |
| v21:V-21.4.0 | Is vert_last really vertical? Last-glyph agreement with the word at the same position one line up vs offsets and page line-order shuffle (30x) | ZL 0.288 (offset -2/-1/+1/+2: 0.278/0.287/0.285/0.286; 2 lines up 0.281; 4 lines up 0.287); page line-shuffle 0.275 +- 0.002 (z 6.4). IT2a 0.295 vs 0.281 (z 5.9) | Not vertical: neighbouring lines share their word-final glyph mix whatever the column. It is paragraph-scale clumping of line make-up (cf. N1), not a grid |
| v21:V-21.4.1 | F11 vs Voynich ZL3b and IT2a; controls | ZL 0.926/0.927, IT2a 0.932/0.918; null 0.524/0.503; negative 0.675/0.641; planted 0.743/0.707 | The four targeted survivors are gone (vert_last, mg_line_var, first_eq_lag1; para_first_in_rest in ZL, still -5.2 in IT2a) but the AUC does not move (0.934 -> 0.926) |
| v21:V-21.4.2 | What surfaces instead (all 5 fresh seeds |z|>=4, controls |z|<3.5) | ZL: near_far +8.2, u_p -5.8, wlen_ac1 +5.1, CS_switch -4.6. IT2a: near_far +9.3, u_p -6.0, para_first_in_rest -5.2, last_eq_lag2 +4.9, wlen_ac1 +4.5 | Whack-a-mole. wlen_ac1 returns because F11's key dropped the length bucket (F10 had it and closed it); u_p is F11's own artefact (para-first tables over-produce p). New and persistent: near_far (edit-1 variants 2+ lines apart, survivor in cycles 1, 3 IT2a, 4 both) and CS_switch (ch-words and sh-words come in runs, cf. v12) |
| v21:V-21.4.3 | Positive herbals vs F11 | Latin 0.926 (floor 0.581): vert_last -10.4, page_hapax -7.3, rep_any +7.2, ttr -7.2, rep_line2_4 +7.1, rep_otherpara +6.4, jacc_paras +6.0, para_first_in_rest +5.9. Italian 0.904 (floor 0.773; F11's m/g rule makes artefacts on Italian u_m/u_g): wlen_ac2 +9.9, mg_line_var +7.7, rep_adj -6.7 | Real text remains caught by topic re-use whatever local rules are added. The Voynich never is: in 4 forger generations no topic feature (ttr, rep_line2_4, rep_otherpara, jacc_paras, rep_rare) survives, and its paragraph-first line is re-used LESS than forged where the herbals' is re-used MORE |
| v22:V-22.1.0 | First pass with FACTORISED emissions (first unit, last unit, length bin as independent factors) | Every model with latent stages gained 140-215 mb/token in all corpora; Latin with words shuffled inside paragraphs kept 176 of its 179 | ARTEFACT, caught by the word-shuffle null: latent stages were modelling the dependence between factors of the SAME word, not order. Discarded; everything below uses one joint word class |
| v22:V-22.1.1 | ZL3b real, gains over M0 (mb/token) | best rigid 28 (head/body/tail), linear drift 8.5, mixture 93, program H2 44, H3 78, H4 101, H6 105, H9 99 | The program beats every rigid model by ~77 mb/token, but most of that is paragraph heterogeneity (order-free mixture alone 93) |
| v22:V-22.1.2 | NULL lines shuffled (n=3) / words shuffled within line-position slots (n=3) | Program 71 +- 3 / 69 +- 3; rigid -1 / -1; mixture 98 / 98. Order information = real - null: program +33 (z 11) / +36 (z 11); rigid +29 / +29 | Order matters (z ~11), but the elastic program extracts only 5-7 mb/token more order than rigid head/body/tail bins (null sd ~3): no clear elastic multi-stage program |
| v22:V-22.1.3 | NULL first line kept, lines 2..n shuffled (n=2) | Program 106 +- 1 vs real 105 (order info -1, z -1.4); rigid 21 vs 28 (+6) | KEY: once the paragraph's first line is in place, shuffling all other lines costs the program NOTHING. The Voynich "program" is two-stage: head line, then an unordered body (plus a weak rigid body drift of ~6 mb, the v19 gradient) |
| v22:V-22.1.4 | NEGATIVE Markov resynthesis (v21 F3: section x position junction tables, own first-line tables) (n=2) | Program 95 +- 5, rigid 14, mixture 84; real exceeds it by 9 (program), 14 (rigid) | The resynthesis already reproduces most of the head-line effect and heterogeneity; small residual |
| v22:V-22.1.5 | PLANTED 4-stage elastic program (Dirichlet(2) durations, tilt 1.0) over 25% / 50% of words, vs paired sham (tilt 0) | plant - sham: rho .25 program +3, rigid +6; rho .5 program +25, rigid +27; stage recovery ARI 0.03 / 0.14 | Plants are detected (rho .5) but the rigid bins catch a moderately elastic plant as well as the program does, and stages are not recovered against Voynich heterogeneity: the 'beyond rigid' test has low power for programs whose stages track relative position |
| v22:V-22.1.6 | POSITIVE Italian herbal (Brumati) | Real program 101 (H9) vs rigid 17, mixture -1; line-shuffled 9 +- 2, word-shuffled -2. Order info: program +91 (z 50), rigid +19 -> +73 beyond rigid | PASSES strongly: a real herbal has elastic ordered structure that rigid position cannot express (cycle 3 separates program from local continuity) |
| v22:V-22.1.7 | POSITIVE Latin Isidore XVI-XVII | Real program 5 (H2), rigid 2; nulls -4/-5. Order info program +10 (z 3-5), rigid +4 | Weak: Isidore's encyclopaedic paragraphs have little template at this word-class resolution |
| v22:V-22.1 | Cycle verdict | - | The Voynich paragraph does NOT run a detectable multi-stage program beyond 'head line + body': all order information the elastic alignment finds sits in line 1 (V-22.1.3). Italian herbal paragraphs do show one (+73 mb beyond rigid). Grade B negative for an elastic program across body lines at this power (plants of the v22.1.5 kind would not be seen beyond rigid) |
| v22:V-22.2.1 | In-line word order: ZL with middle words shuffled inside every line, vs that + lines shuffled (n=2) | 103 vs 71.5 (real 105); order across lines +31.5, in-line order +2 | Word order inside lines adds almost nothing at word-class level; all the order the program uses is between lines |
| v22:V-22.2.2 | Same on herbals | IT 88 vs 4 (+84); LA 3 vs -6 | Italian herbal keeps its strong line-order structure; Latin weak |
| v22:V-22.2.3 | PLANTED very elastic program vs sham | rho .5: program +90, rigid +34 -> +56 beyond rigid, stage ARI 0.41; rho .25: program +11, rigid +7 -> +4, ARI 0.01 | The test CAN see an elastic program beyond rigid position when it shapes ~half the words strongly; a weaker one (1 word in 4) is invisible. Power limit stated |
| v22:V-22.2.4 | IT2a replication (n=1 null each) | real 112, line-shuffled 73, word-shuffled 69; order info +39 / +43, rigid +32 | Same as ZL: program extracts only 7-11 mb more order than rigid bins. Replicates in the second transcription |
| v22:V-22.2.5 | Whole pages as units; null = paragraph order shuffled within page | real 106 vs 103 (+3), rigid 8 vs 4; word-shuffled 86 | No page-level program running across paragraphs; page order information is the within-paragraph one |
| v22:V-22.2.6 | Currier A vs B: stage tilts learned on one language (S=6) transferred to held-out paragraphs of the other, vs the same transfer onto line-shuffled targets (n=2) | Order info (real - shuffled): A->A +76, B->A +62; B->B +65, A->B +60 | The program transfers: 80-90% of each language's order information is carried by the other language's program. Shared head-line program, Grade B |
| v22:V-22.2.7 | The program itself: full-data fit S=6, 300 restarts, top-5 stability, stage profiles | Stage 0 = HEAD (3.7% of tokens, rel. pos 0.04, line 0): gallows in 69% of words (others 41-57%), sh- 22.5% (base ~8%), q- 4.5% (base 15.3%), a-/l- 4.5% (base 9.2%), mean length 5.1 (base 4.5). The 5 body stages sit at rel. pos 0.39-0.61, self-loop 0.93-1.00, and split by Currier language (A share 0.03 / 0.12 / 0.26 / 0.91 / 0.93); paragraphs visit 2.7 stages. Restart agreement ARI 0.32-0.53 | Body 'stages' are regimes (language/section), not steps of a sequence; only head -> body is a step. The q-high / a-l-low drift of v19 is NOT a stage sequence: the head is low in q AND low in a/l; the body stages differ by language, not by order |
| v22:V-22.2 | Cycle verdict | - | Voynich paragraphs run a two-stage program at most: a distinctive head line (gallows, sh-, long words, few q- and a-/l- words) then an unordered body. Shared by Currier A and B and by both transcriptions. Grade B |
| v22:V-22.3.1 | Anchoring: forward minus reversed (H6), with line-shuffled null pairs | ZL 105 vs 87 (+19); null pairs +1, -3. Currier A 63 vs 21 (+42, H3 +27); B 74 vs 58 (+16). IT +15; LA +8 (H6) | The Voynich program is anchored at the paragraph START in both languages (a fixed opening, not a fixed closing formula). Strongest in A |
| v22:V-22.3.2 | Rotation null (n=2) vs line shuffle | ZL rotated 72 / 75 = line-shuffled 70-72 (real 105); IT rotated 42 / 35, line-shuffled 7, head-kept 25 (real 82) | Voynich: NO local continuity across lines at word-class level; its whole order signal is positional (the head line). Italian herbal: ~30 mb of continuity (topic runs) AND ~40-47 mb positional program extending beyond its first line. Control behaves as a templated text should |
| v22:V-22.3.3 | Line clock: rate of stage changes at line starts vs elsewhere | ZL 0.010 vs 0.036 (shuffled 0.013-0.033 vs 0.028-0.037); IT 0.044 vs 0.051 | Stage changes are not clocked by line starts (beyond the head line itself, which is a whole line) |
| v22:V-22.3 | Cycle verdict | - | Start-anchored head + positionless body, no continuity across lines; the Italian herbal shows a genuine elastic program plus continuity. Grade B |
| v22:V-22.F0 | Method artefact | Factorised emissions let latent stages model within-word factor dependence (+140-215 mb even with words shuffled) | Caught by the word-shuffle null; replaced by one joint word class. Lesson for any latent-state test |
| v22:V-22.F1 | How many stages beat the null? | Order information ~33 mb/token (z ~11 vs line shuffle) in ZL and IT2a, but the elastic program extracts only 5-11 mb more than rigid head/body/tail bins; head-kept line shuffle costs nothing (-1) | TWO: head line, then body. No multi-stage program across body lines. Grade B (power: a very elastic plant shaping 50% of words is found, +56 beyond rigid; one shaping 25% is not) |
| v22:V-22.F2 | What the stages look like | Head: gallows 69%, sh- 22%, q- 4.5% (base 15%), a-/l- 4.5% (base 9%), long words; body 'stages' are Currier-language regimes, unstable across restarts (ARI 0.3-0.5) | The v19 q-high / a-l-low drift is not explained by a stage sequence; Grade B |
| v22:V-22.F3 | Currier A vs B | Each language's program carries 80-90% of the other's order information; both start-anchored (A +42, B +16 forward over reversed) | Shared program (= the head-line convention). Grade B |
| v22:V-22.F4 | Positive control | Italian herbal: +73 mb beyond rigid, survives rotation (~40 mb positional) and extends past line 1 | Method finds a real template; the Voynich lacks one beyond its opening line |
| v22:V-22.F | Overall | - | The Voynich paragraph is NOT a recipe-like program: it is an opening line followed by order-free text, with no cross-line continuity. Not cracked; nothing read; nothing predicted outside the corpus. Grade C guess: the head line is a formal label/opening convention, would support: head-line words recur as page or section keys; would kill: head-line class profile matched by a body line chosen at random in held-out pages |

| v25:V-25 | THE GLYPHS ARE BUILT FROM FEATURES: Voynich glyphs decomposed into stroke primitives (hand, fixed before statistics) and into font-image descriptors (Landini EVA font, no hand decomposition); Mantel of shape vs behaviour (PPMI, SVD, pseudolikelihood Potts) against random glyph-to-shape assignment, 3,000 random and 3,000 perturbed decompositions, primitive-subset search with held-out half; swap pairs; parallelograms; leave-one-glyph-out compositional prediction. Controls: Hangul jamo (positive), Latin (3 fonts) and Greek (negative), planted featural and arbitrary Latin-in-Voynich-shapes, misreading noise, ZL-IT consensus text, position-profile partial, 31 copy-and-edit generators | see rows | see rows |
| v25:V-25.F1 | Does glyph shape predict glyph behaviour beyond the null? | Mantel (shape similarity vs context similarity), null = random glyph-to-shape assignment. Hand strokes: Voynich ZL r +0.39, IT +0.38 (z ~5; beats 100% of 3,000 random decompositions; frequency-stratified and partial controls hold). Font-image shape (no hand decomposition): ZL +0.28/+0.43 (ppmi/Potts), IT +0.29/+0.44, Currier A +0.31/+0.41, B +0.29/+0.42, all p at floor. Hangul +0.53 hand / +0.25-0.33 image. Latin (3 fonts) and Greek: -0.05 to +0.06, null. Planted featural Latin-in-Voynich-shapes +0.44 to +0.49; arbitrary assignments mean 0.00 | YES. The Voynich behaves like Hangul (featural), not like Latin or Greek (arbitrary). Grade A |
| v25:V-25.F2 | Is it an artefact? | Misreading: ~10% shape-driven confusion needed for r +0.11, ZL-IT disagree on 1.1%, consensus text keeps the effect. Position: partial r controlling word-position profile still +0.25 (image) / +0.28 (hand). Copy-and-edit generator from shape-neutral seeds: 0/27 settings reproduce image r, hand r and the bench parallelogram together | Misreading and positional class killed as explanations; stroke-edit copying explains part, not all. Grade B |
| v25:V-25.F3 | Is it compositional (featural)? | Leave-one-glyph-out: an unseen glyph's behaviour predicted from its strokes, cosine +0.23 ZL / +0.26 IT (p 0.002), like Hangul +0.24; Latin, Greek null. Parallelograms: +bench (k->ckh, t->cth, p->cph, f->cfh) offset cosine +0.95 (p 0.0003, ZL and IT); +WIDE crossbar (k->f, t->p ...) Potts +0.59 (p 0.0007) | Two additive stroke features: the bench and the p/f crossbar. Grade B |
| v25:V-25.F4 | Which strokes carry it (candidate features)? | Hand: gallows LEGS +0.38, bench BAR +0.31, c-CURVE +0.27, minim, tails; closed LOOP carries nothing (or hurts). Image: ascender height +0.25-0.30 in every set; width +0.16-0.23; holes ~0. Half-split subset search replicates (held-out r +0.52 ZL, +0.49 IT) | Candidate features: TALL (gallows/ascender), BENCH (c-c bar), MINIM and TAIL classes. Grade C as sound features: would support if a reading assigns one sound class per stroke class and predicts held-out words; would kill if a generator with shape-driven edits matches F1-F3 from shape-neutral seeds |
| v25:V-25.F5 | Do the tandem swap pairs differ by one stroke? | Swap-excess vs shape: ZL r +0.02 hand / -0.02 image; named pairs k/t, l/o, ch/sh at one stroke (3/7 vs 8% of pairs) but image percentiles mean 63 (5/7 above median, p 0.23) | NO beyond chance. Grade C, demoted |
| v25:V-25.F | Overall | - | SHAPE PREDICTS BEHAVIOUR. Voynich glyphs that share strokes behave alike, as strongly as Hangul jamo and unlike Latin or Greek letters, under font-image shape with no hand decomposition, in both transcriptions and both Currier languages, and compositionally (a new glyph's behaviour is predicted from its strokes; the bench and p/f crossbar act as additive features). Misreading and position do not explain it; stroke-edit copying explains part. The script is designed (or evolved) featurally; whether the features encode sound or a generator's edit rules is open. Not cracked |
| v25:V-25.1.V1 | Verdict on V-25.1.1-2 (does stroke shape predict behaviour?) | Voynich ZL r +0.39 / IT +0.38 (z ~5, p at floor, survives frequency-stratified null and frequency partial), beats 100% of 3,000 random decompositions, robust to 1-5 flipped entries (5th pct +0.33). Hangul +0.53 (z 14). Latin +0.04 (null). Greek +0.15 (p 0.02, gone under the frequency-stratified null: p 0.59) | POSITIVE CONTROL PASSES (Hangul), NEGATIVE PASSES (Latin; Greek marginal and frequency-driven). Voynich sits between Hangul and the arbitrary alphabets, near Hangul. Caveat: the hand decomposition was written by someone who knows Voynich glyph behaviour; cycle 2 replaces it with font-image shape |
| v25:V-25.1.V2 | Verdict on V-25.1.3-4 (which strokes carry it) | Single carriers: LEGS (gallows) +0.38, BAR (bench) +0.31, CURVE +0.27, TLOOP +0.18, WIDE +0.14; drop-one losses CURVE, MINIM, LEGS; LOOP hurts (dropping it gains +0.08). Half-split search: best subset on half A (ASC,CURL,CURVE,DESC,FLAG,LEGS,MINIM,PLUME,RTAIL) gives held-out r +0.52 (ZL) / +0.49 (IT), p 0.0002; Latin's best subset (TALL,...) also replicates at +0.21 | Shape classes that carry behaviour: gallows legs, c-curve/bench, minim, tails. Closed LOOP is NOT a behavioural feature (o, y, d, l, q differ in behaviour). Grade B (subset search replicates on held-out half and in IT) |
| v25:V-25.1.V3 | Verdict on V-25.1.5 (parallelograms) | Voynich: +bench (k->ckh, t->cth, p->cph, f->cfh) offset cosine +0.95 svd / +0.79 Potts, p 0.0003 both, ZL and IT; +WIDE (k->f, t->p, ckh->cfh, cth->cph) Potts +0.59 p 0.0007; +TLOOP, +PLUME, +RTAIL null. Hangul: +i vowel family +0.48 (p 0.01), y-vowel Potts +0.36 (p 0.01), aspiration/doubling weak. Latin/Greek: none | Two Voynich strokes act as additive features: the bench (benched gallows = gallows + ch-like context shift, consistently) and the WIDE crossbar of p/f (a consistent shift, which is the known paragraph-initial role of p/f). Grade B for 'bench and WIDE are additive behavioural features'; caveat: a two-cluster structure (gallows vs benched gallows) also makes offsets parallel |
| v25:V-25.1.V4 | Verdict on V-25.1.6-7 (swap pairs) | Swap-excess vs stroke similarity: ZL r +0.02 (p 0.36), IT +0.09 (p 0.08); Latin, Greek, Hangul ~0. Named v5/v12 pairs: 3/7 at stroke distance 1 (k/t, l/o, ch/sh) vs 8% of all pairs (binomial tail ~0.014), but l/r d4, ch/k d5, ch/cth d3, a/o d3 | Swap pairs as a whole do NOT follow stroke distance (Mantel null). The one-stroke share among named pairs is high but rests on 3 pairs and an author-made decomposition. Grade C; re-tested with image shape in cycle 2 |
| v25:V-25.1.V5 | Verdict on V-25.1.8-10 (planted) | Latin sound features predict Latin letter behaviour r +0.61. Latin written in Voynich shapes by a featural assignment: r +0.49 (p 0.0005); 40 arbitrary assignments: mean -0.005, 5% at p<0.05 | Pipeline recovers a planted featural script and stays calibrated on arbitrary ones. The Voynich (+0.39) is at 80% of the planted featural level |
| v25:V-25.2.V1 | Verdict: does shape predict behaviour with NO hand decomposition? | Font-image combo vs behaviour: Voynich ZL r +0.28/+0.30/+0.43 (ppmi/svd/Potts), IT +0.29/+0.26/+0.44, Currier A +0.31/+0.32/+0.41, Currier B +0.29/+0.27/+0.42, all p at the 0.0002 floor and under the frequency-stratified null. Hangul +0.25/+0.31/+0.33. Latin under 3 fonts -0.05 to +0.05; Greek -0.02 to +0.06 (all p > 0.1) | SURVIVES the contamination control: shape from Landini's EVA font predicts behaviour as strongly as Hangul jamo shape does, in both transcriptions and both Currier languages; arbitrary alphabets show nothing. Grade A for 'Voynich glyph shape predicts glyph distribution far beyond arbitrary alphabets' (replicated: ZL, IT, A, B; 2 independent shape sources; frequency-controlled) |
| v25:V-25.2.V2 | Verdict: which automatic stroke feature carries it | Ascender height r +0.28/+0.30/+0.29/+0.25 (ZL/IT/A/B, p ~0.001); width +0.16-0.23; holes (closed loops) ~0 everywhere; Hangul: descender +0.50, ascender +0.41 (vowel vs consonant geometry) | The Voynich signal is carried by TALL strokes (gallows legs, d/l ascenders) and width (benched glyphs), not by loops. Candidate 'feature' = tall vs short = a gallows/consonant-like class. Grade B |
| v25:V-25.2.V3 | Verdict: swap pairs | Swap excess vs image similarity: ZL r -0.02, IT +0.04, controls ~0. Named pairs' image-similarity percentiles: k/t 79, l/r 82, l/o 43, ch/cth 66, ch/sh 68, ch/k 10, a/o 94 (mean 63; 5/7 above median, sign-test p 0.23) | Tandem swap pairs are NOT preferentially one-stroke apart beyond chance. Grade C 'swaps are shape-near' -> demoted |
| v25:V-25.2.V4 | Verdict: planted (image) | Featural assignment r +0.44 (p 0.0005); arbitrary mean -0.003 sd 0.07 | Calibrated; Voynich Potts r +0.43 equals the planted featural level |
| v25:V-25.3.V1 | Verdict K1 (misreading) | Shape-driven misreading noise in an arbitrary-shape Latin script needs ~10% of glyphs misread to reach r +0.11; ZL-IT disagree on 1.1% of glyphs; the consensus text (1,809 lines where ZL and IT agree glyph for glyph) keeps image r +0.26, hand r +0.38 | KILLED as an explanation: transcriber or scribal misreading of similar shapes cannot make the effect |
| v25:V-25.3.V2 | Verdict K2 (copy-and-edit generator) | Seeded with real Voynich words, shape-driven edits keep or raise the effect (image +0.46, bench +0.87) - inherited from the seeds. From relabelled seeds (no initial link), shape-driven edits at 70% per word build image r +0.20 but hand r +0.03 and no parallelogram (bench +0.16, p 0.13); shape-blind edits give nothing. Generator swap Mantel -0.19 vs Voynich ~0 | A stroke-edit copying process CAN create part of the link (image level), so 'sound features' is not the only mechanism; but from scratch it does not reach the Voynich's hand-level r or the bench parallelogram. Not killed; tuned in cycle 4 |
| v25:V-25.3.V3 | Verdict K3 (position) | Partial r controlling for word-position profile: Voynich image +0.28 -> +0.25, hand +0.39 -> +0.28 (p 0.001); Hangul +0.53 -> +0.48; Latin, Greek ~0 | Not a pure positional-class effect: most of it survives with position removed |
| v25:V-25.3.V4 | Verdict K4 (compositional) | Leave-one-glyph-out: behaviour of an unseen glyph predicted from its strokes: Voynich ZL cosine +0.23 (hand) / +0.16 (image), IT +0.26 / +0.21, Hangul +0.24 / +0.14, all p <= 0.01; Latin, Greek null | The Voynich behaves like Hangul: a new glyph's distribution is predictable from its strokes. Grade B for 'compositional featural structure' |
| v25:V-25.4.V | Verdict: can stroke-edit copying from a shape-neutral start reproduce the Voynich? 27 settings | 0/27 settings match image r, hand r and bench at once; closest k5 e0.3 w100: image +0.217, hand +0.183, bench +0.44, TTR 0.17 |  |
| v25:V-25.4.V2 | Verdict, cycle 4 | One generator run per setting, so single values are noisy (image r ranges -0.08 to +0.53). The generator builds the image-level link it is driven by but not the independent hand-level link (max +0.34) together with the bench parallelogram (max +0.62 vs Voynich +0.95) | Stroke-edit copying from a shape-neutral start does not reproduce the Voynich's full featural profile in 27 settings. Not a proof that no generator can: a generator seeded with real Voynich words inherits it (V-25.3.3) |
| r3:R-3.final | Grow the script in a box: 62,000 simulated societies (ledger or text records; scripts evolved by iterated learning with bottleneck, logogram cap, abbreviation, drift, scribes, secrecy, dividers, end sets, line markers), 2,500-sign corpora, 20-22 blind statistics, rejection ABC + local-linear adjustment, 1 refinement round. Controls: pseudo-data identifiability; Ur III, LB, Latin, Italian known worlds (full and number-blind panels); sign-redealt corpora; held-out statistics fixed before each cycle (loops/r3_cycle1-3.txt, r3_final.txt) | VMS fits a text world: no numbers, lines of ~9-11 words, ~15-25 signs, long words of tight length, ~every word ends in a 1-4-sign end set, ~30-40% of lines open with a marker sign (marker rate identifiable, r 0.94; Latin 0.03, shuffled 0.02). Box-1 'secrecy needed' (0.82) fell to 0.06-0.14 once positional grammar was available. After refinement the real VMS (3.68) beats its shuffle (4.61) but stays just outside the cloud; unreproduced: cross-word junction coupling (+4.7 sd). Held-out: 1/4 pass (line-final KL), purity miss, 2 inside but uninformative | Secrecy guess KILLED. 'Small alphabet + end set + line-initial marker' C (restates known position effects; new number: marker rate ~0.3-0.4). Not cracked. |
| r3:R-3.1a | 24,000 random worlds from the prior (30 parameters), each grows a script by iterated learning and writes a 2,500-sign corpus; panel of 20 blind statistics; rejection ABC (nearest 1% = 240, Epanechnikov weights, local-linear adjustment in logit space). Control C5: 300 held-out simulations as pseudo-data | Recovery r >= 0.7: p_ledger 0.90, p_num 0.78, q_mu 0.92, p_round 0.80, text_len 0.83, lg_epd 0.99, lg_syl 0.91, wlen 0.89, lg_logo 0.78, secrecy 0.90, p_div 0.94. Not identifiable (r < 0.5): n_people 0.39, n_goods 0.38, Zipf exponents, topicality 0.17, n_gen 0.09, bottleneck 0.27, abbreviation 0.16, drift 0.12, n_scribes 0.24, shared 0.56. Pseudo-data nearest distance median 1.88, 95th pct 2.64. Held-out stats 90% coverage on pseudo-data: burst 0.90, adj_rep 0.96 (calibrated) | Box works as an instrument for 11 parameters. The iterated-learning history (generations, bottleneck, drift, scribes) and the people/goods sizes are NOT recoverable from a 2,500-sign sample, so no claim is made about them for any script. |
| r3:R-3.1b | Known-world controls C1-C3 (full panel) | Ur III: p_ledger 0.82 [0.64-0.97] PASS; people_over_goods -0.06 FAIL (lg_people unidentifiable, see 1a); dmin 2.63 (95th pct of pseudo-data, at the edge). Linear B: p_ledger 0.86 [0.61-0.97] PASS; dmin 3.57 (outside the cloud). Latin (Caesar): p_ledger 0.05 [0.01-0.19] PASS, text_len 11.6, p_div 0.95, lg_syl 1.13 (13 signs), logograms ~1. Italian (Dante): p_ledger 0.30 [0.03-0.59] PASS (borderline) | Ledger vs text is recovered on all four known corpora. The 'many people, few goods' signature of Ur III is NOT recovered (the instrument cannot see it). |
| r3:R-3.1c | Shuffle control C4: signs redealt over each corpus (3 seeds; word lengths, entries, numbers kept) | Nearest distance real vs shuffled: PE 4.21 vs 4.29-4.44 (pass, small); LA 2.81 vs 3.28-3.38 (pass); VMS 7.39 vs 5.91-6.05 (FAIL: shuffled fits better); Ur III 2.63 vs 2.11-2.25 (FAIL); LB 3.57 vs 2.72-2.79 (FAIL); Latin 3.97 vs 5.03-5.25 (pass). Posterior moves under shuffle: Ur III p_ledger 0.82 -> 0.26-0.38; VMS p_ledger 0.90 -> 0.37-0.49, wlen 4.4 -> 1.4; LA lg_logo 1.76 -> 0.02, secrecy 0.05 -> 0.25-0.37; PE little change | The box is misspecified for Ur III, LB and the Voynich: the real corpora carry structure no simulated world makes, and a shuffled copy sits closer to the cloud. | v23:V-23.1.1 | Glyph-level entropy production (lag 1, 2, 4 on the glyph stream), unsigned | Excess bits lag 1: Voynich 4.44 / 4.50 / 4.31 vs languages 1.46-2.45; lag 2: 2.03 / 2.06 vs 0.33-0.94; lag 4: 0.43-0.46 vs 0.09-0.16. MkG 4.39 / 2.03 / 0.42 (reproduced); RvG 0.000-0.001 (null works) | The Voynich glyph stream is 2-4x MORE time-irreversible than any language, and an order-3 glyph Markov chain reproduces all of it: it is the rigid one-way glyph order inside words (q-first, -y/-n/-m last), a local rule, not a sign of language or its absence. Grade A (descriptive) |
| v23:V-23.1.2 | Word-level entropy production (top-300 types + other, lag 1 and 2) | Lag 1 excess 0.10 / 0.08 / 0.07 (z 25 / 20 / 14) vs languages 0.30-0.66 (z 52-74). LAG 2: 0.002 / 0.005 / 0.001 (z 0.4 / 1.3 / 0.1) vs 0.051-0.160 (z 13-29) in all five languages including Hebrew; planted 0.12-0.14; MkW (word bigram chain of the Voynich) 0.037 (z 9); RvW 0.000-0.003 | (DEMOTED in V-23.2.1: mostly the function-word gap.) Two words apart, the Voynich has NO arrow of time at all, in both transcriptions and at matched size, while every language (inflecting, verse, Semitic) has a strong one, and even a bigram Markov chain built from Voynich words has more. Adjacent-word arrow 3-7x weaker than languages (extends v11 V-4.1). Must survive the function-word control (cycle 2) |
| v23:V-23.1.3 | Word-length and word-frequency series: ordinal-pattern and horizontal-visibility-graph irreversibility (unsigned) and signed ordinal (rising minus falling) | Ord-freq z 0.0 / 1.1 / -0.4, HVG-freq 1.7 / 0.3 / 3.4 vs LA 25 / 21, ITA 64 / 47, CS 19 / 10 but DE 1.5 / 0.4, HE 0.2 / 2.3. Signed ord-freq: Voynich +0.6 / +1.2 vs LA -6.1, ITA -8.7, CS -5.6, DE -1.8, HE -0.9; planted reversed +7.1 / +7.9 (flipped) | The frequency-series arrow is a function-word arrow (frequent word, then rare word) present in Latin/Italian/Czech, weak in German and absent in Hebrew (clitic prefixes). Voynich behaves like Hebrew/German here: not diagnostic. Grade B (descriptive) |
| v23:V-23.1.4 | Held-out Kneser-Ney forward vs backward cross-entropy (glyph stream orders 2/4/6, within-word order 4, junction chain, word bigram/trigram, length and first+last-glyph classes, line codes); 5-fold over pages | Effects are 1e-6 to 4e-3 bits/symbol everywhere (the n-gram arrow is tiny, as in the literature). Within-word order 4: Voynich -2.8 / -0.6 / -3.6 vs LA +4.4, CS +4.5, ITA +1.3, DE +0.9, HE 0.0; PL_REV -3.4 (correctly flipped). Word bigram: Voynich +2.1 / +1.8 / +0.3, LA +3.2, ITA +2.8, DE +2.3, CS -3.4, HE +0.2; MkW +2.4 | Words read backwards are slightly easier to predict in ZL (z -2.8, -3.6 at matched size) but NOT in IT2a (-0.6): transcription-dependent, grade C (the within-word right-to-left layering is Parisel's prior claim, not ours). Word-order model arrow is weak and has the language sign (+), but MkW shows a word bigram chain gives the same: no evidence beyond adjacent pairs |
| v23:V-23.1.5 | Compression of the reversed page (lzma, zlib), bits/char rev - fwd | lzma z: Voynich -2.2 / -2.3 / -1.7; languages +1.3 / +0.5 / +5.1 / +3.4 / +5.5 (all five +); HEvis -3.9 and PL_REV -3.4 (flipped, as they must be); MkG -3.3 | Script-direction check PASSES: Hebrew in logical order reads forward, in visual order backward, so the method finds the arrow whatever the writing direction. The Voynich compresses slightly BETTER BACKWARDS (opposite sign to every language, same as the planted reversed text), but the effect is carried by an order-3 glyph chain (MkG -3.3), so it is word-internal glyph order, not discourse. Grade C ('reads as if written backwards' at glyph level only; would support: same sign in a third transcription and in Currier A and B separately; would kill: sign flips by section) |
| v23:V-23.1.6 | Introduction-then-reuse: mean over repeated types of (first + last relative position - 1), per page and per paragraph | Voynich page +3.7 / +3.0 / +3.1, paragraph +4.9 / +4.9 / +3.2; languages -1.8 to +0.5; MkG +0.2 / +1.2, MkW -0.9 / +2.1; RvG max 2.5 | The ONLY discourse-like asymmetry in the Voynich, absent from all five languages: words recur in spans shifted toward the END of paragraphs (vocabulary 'arrives' and stays). Could be the paragraph-initial line (gallows, unique words). KILLED in V-23.2.3 (paragraph-initial line) |
| v23:V-23.1.7 | Positional trends (word length within lines; line length within paragraphs) | Word length falls along the line: Voynich -6.5 / -7.1, also LA -3.9, DE -4.8, HE -3.9 but ITA +3.4, CS +10.5; line length falls through the paragraph: Voynich -9.6 / -9.4 vs languages -1.0 to -6.7 | Layout asymmetries (long line-initial words, short last lines), known; not time-irreversibility in the dynamic sense. Recorded, not graded |
| v23:V-23.1.8 | Mutation arrow: near-copies (edit distance 1) within 5 words: is the later member rarer / longer? | Rarer: Voynich -0.8 / -1.1 / -0.0; longer: -0.4 / -1.6 / +0.6; languages mostly |z|<2 (HE +5.6 length, CS +2.3 rarity) | NO copy-then-mutate arrow: in a self-citation generator the later copy should be the derived (rarer) form. Upper bound: not visible at |z| ~2 over ~2,000 near pairs. Weak evidence against forward self-citation as the generator (grade C negative) |
| v23:V-23.1.V | Cycle-1 verdict | Calibrated (reversible surrogates sd 1.06); script-direction check passed; planted reversals flipped | The Voynich has a STRONG arrow inside words (rigid glyph order, 2-4x any language, Markov-local) and essentially NONE between words beyond adjacent pairs (lag 2 null, languages z 13-29). Direction of the weak signs: glyph level points backwards (compression, within-word model, ZL only), word level points forwards like languages but no more than a bigram chain. The one non-local asymmetry is the late-shifted reuse span inside paragraphs |
| v23:V-23.2.1 | Does the missing lag-2 word arrow only reflect missing function words? | Lag-2 excess (z): ZL 0.0025 (0.7), IT 0.0059 (1.6), ZL19 0.0008 (0.2). Languages raw 0.055-0.161 (13-31); strip50: LA 0.0080 (3.4), ITA 0.0128 (4.5), DE 0.0117 (3.0), CS 0.0229 (7.5), HE 0.0412 (11.6); glue30: 0.0099-0.0320 (4.8-10.1); shufl: all <= 0.008 (z <= 2.2), ZL shufl -0.003 | MOSTLY YES. Removing function words cuts the language lag-2 arrow 4-14x, to within 1.4-7x of the Voynich. A residue survives: every stripped or glued language (11-19k tokens) still shows z 3.0-11.6, while the Voynich at 35k tokens shows 0.7 / 1.6. Lag 1: stripped languages 0.060-0.123, Voynich 0.073-0.101 (the same). DEMOTED from headline to grade C: 'beyond the adjacent word the Voynich is at least as time-symmetric as content-word sequences of the most symmetric language tested' (would support: the same in a third transcription and with Latin/Italian at 35k tokens stripped; would kill: a stripped language with z < 2 at matched size) |
| v23:V-23.2.2 | Class-level (frequency-rank bins) entropy production, lags 1-3 | C1: Voynich 0.0003 / 0.0001 (z 0.7 / 0.2) vs languages 0.004-0.032 (z 4.9-39); stripped 0.001-0.008. C3: Voynich 0.0017 / 0.0019 (z 4.1 / 4.9), languages -0.001 to +0.002 (z <= 2.8), MkW 2.5, ZL shufl 1.2 | No frequency-class arrow at lag 1 in the Voynich (languages: function word, then content word). An unexplained small lag-3 frequency-class arrow in both transcriptions (z 4-5): grade C, probably a line-position gradient (lines ~8 words); not followed further |
| v23:V-23.2.3 | Introduction-then-reuse (cycle-1 V-23.1.6) with paragraph edge lines removed | ZL full +4.9, drop LAST line +4.7, drop FIRST line -0.2, both -0.8; IT +4.9 / +4.6 / -0.7 / -1.0; languages -2.3 to +1.1 | KILLED. The late-shifted reuse spans come entirely from the paragraph-initial line (its words do not recur). Not a discourse arrow; the known first-line effect |
| v23:V-23.2.4 | Neural forward vs backward glyph predictor (held-out pages), bits/glyph bwd - fwd, 2 seeds | Languages 10/10 runs positive: LA +0.029 / +0.019, ITA +0.008 / +0.020, DE +0.009 / +0.012, CS +0.016 / +0.046, HE +0.005 / +0.019 (relative +0.2% to +1.7%); planted reversed -0.025 / -0.031 (flipped); MkG -0.000 / +0.001; RvGL -0.001. Voynich ZL -0.0075 / +0.0045, IT +0.0136 / +0.0027 (relative -0.4% to +0.7%) | The forward-is-easier arrow known for language models appears in all five languages and flips for the planted reversal. The Voynich gives a weaker and seed-dependent sign (mean +0.003 vs languages +0.019; two of four runs below every language run). Method warning: the per-page sign-flip z (ZL -5.1 vs +2.8 for two seeds) ignores model-to-model variance and is not valid for single neural fits. Grade C: 'Voynich glyph stream has a weaker neural arrow than any language' |
| v23:V-23.2.V | Cycle-2 verdict | | The word-level gap with languages is mostly the function-word gap (Hebrew-like); a small residue remains (grade C). The only discourse-looking asymmetry was the paragraph first line (killed). Neural predictors find the language arrow everywhere and a weak, unstable one in the Voynich |
| v23:V-23.3.1 | Glyph level, 2,000 random glyph-partition probes | Replicated: ZL 1392, IT 1421 vs languages 896-1248, MkG 1613; random-direction null 0 in every corpus; held-out corr(z_A, z_B) 0.97-0.99 everywhere (null -0.8 to +0.2) | Every corpus is massively irreversible at glyph level; the Voynich slightly more, and its own order-3 glyph chain more still. Not diagnostic (confirms V-23.1.1) |
| v23:V-23.3.2 | Word IDENTITY arrows: random partitions of word types (~400 probes per corpus) | ZL 7/375, IT 10/417; LA 0/428, ITA 0/400, DE 0/390, HE 0/401; CS 107/414 (verse); PL_REV 1; MkW 8/399, MkG 6/396; RvW 0; null 0 | Random groupings of words almost never carry an arrow in prose of any language (0/400), a little in the Voynich (2%, both transcriptions), and the same amount in its own Markov resyntheses: the Voynich identity arrows are local (adjacent-word/junction) rules, not discourse |
| v23:V-23.3.3 | Word CLASS arrows: first-glyph, last-glyph, length, frequency classes | ZL first 160/428, last 137/405, length 170/414, FREQUENCY 0/378; IT 158 / 144 / 152 / 3. Languages: first 0-5, last 10-23, length 24-47, frequency 48-73 (CS 161-368; HE 2 / 3 / 48 / 0). MkW 181 / 173 / 142 / 15; MkG 154 / 118 / 97 / 28; null 0 | Two opposite profiles. Languages: arrows ride on FREQUENCY (function word, then content word), weakly on shape. Voynich: arrows ride on SHAPE (first/last glyph, length: the junction rule and line-position gradients) and NEVER on frequency (0/378, cf. Hebrew 0/380 whose function words are prefixes). Every shape arrow is reproduced by a word-bigram or glyph-trigram chain. Grade A (descriptive): the Voynich word arrow is a spelling arrow, not a syntax arrow |
| v23:V-23.3.4 | Line chains (first word of line i -> line i+1; last words likewise) | ZL first-word 31, length 44, frequency 46 (IT 38 / 38 / 45); languages 0-23 (LA 23 on length); MkW 30 / 10 / 37; MkG 30 / 3 / 23. The random-page-direction null is NOT valid at this level (reversal swaps the first-word and last-word chains; null gives 20-45), so only the sign-flip z within the real text applies | Line-to-line arrows exist in the Voynich (e.g. gallows-initial lines precede ch-initial lines: the paragraph-initial line), are reproduced by Markov chains that keep the paragraph token, and are a layout marker. Not graded beyond 'known paragraph-initial effect' |
| v23:V-23.3.V | Cycle-3 verdict | Null 0 survivors at glyph and word level in all 7 null runs; positive controls find hundreds | Thousands of random hypotheses agree with cycles 1-2: the Voynich has abundant arrows of SPELLING (glyph order, word junction, line position) and almost none of SYNTAX or DISCOURSE (frequency-class arrows 0/378 in ZL vs 48-73 in Latin/Italian/German). What arrows it has are all reproduced by Markov chains of order <= 3 glyphs / 1 word |

| v29:V-29.F | Overall | - | FEATURES DO NOT SPREAD. The Voynich's featural glyph design (v25) does not show the agreement that featural phonology produces in harmony languages; its junction dependence is a word-class pairing (y-words before q-words, n-words before o/ch-words) with same-glyph avoidance and no sandhi. If strokes encode sounds, the language has no harmony or assimilation visible in writing; the simpler reading is that strokes encode slot/position classes of a word grammar. Not cracked |

| v29:V-29.2.S | Cycle summary | - | No Voynich stroke feature spreads through words like a harmony feature. The large junction effect is one glyph-level pairing (word-final y/g -> word-initial q/y; and m/g vs n), robust across halves and transcriptions, and stronger than matched glyph classes, but whether it is FEATURAL (shared strokes attract) or POSITIONAL (a fixed end-start rule) is open -> cycle 3 |
| v29:V-29.3.1 | KILL CONTROL: shape-neutral copy-and-edit generator (each word copied with prob 0.5 / 0.8 from the previous 12 words, Poisson 1 / 1.5 random glyph substitutions, else a corpus word; line lengths kept), full cycle-1 search | copy5: junc 0 features above the corrected threshold (of 2,048), within-word 0; copy8: junc 0, raw3 1 stroke / 7 class. Voynich ZL: junc 390 stroke + 70 class | Copying with edits from nearby words does not make junction agreement (the within-line shuffle keeps copy relations). The Voynich junction effect is not a by-product of generic self-citation. PASS for the control |
| v29:V-29.3.2 | SANDHI DIRECTION: REG = I(final1; initial2 \| rest of word 1), PROG = I(initial2; final1 \| rest of word 2), excess over 20 within-line shuffles, also divided by H(final \| stem) or H(initial \| rest). Planted sandhi controls (prob 0.5 Latin / 0.3 Voynich) | Planted regressive: Latin REG +0.096 vs natural +0.030 (PROG flat); Voynich REG +0.086 (z 20) vs PROG +0.019. Planted progressive: Voynich PROG +0.045 (z 21) vs REG +0.005; Latin PROG +0.024 vs +0.011. Natural: Latin REG/H 0.072, PROG/H 0.046; Turkish 0.035/0.106; Hungarian 0.027/0.035; Czech 0.042/0.061. Voynich ZL 0.005/0.008 (glyph), 0.017/0.016 (stroke classes); IT 0.010/0.009; Currier A -0.021/-0.007; B 0.007/0.003. Copy generators ~0 | Both directions recovered when planted. In the real Voynich, holding the rest of a word fixed, its final glyph hardly varies with the next word's initial, nor its initial with the previous final: 4-20x less than in any control language. NO SANDHI: the junction dependence is between whole word types, not an edge that adapts. Grade B |
| v29:V-29.3.3 | SIMILARITY ATTRACTION (feature-free): weighted correlation of log(obs/expected) for glyph pairs (junction; 2 and 3 apart in a word) with their feature similarity (weighted Jaccard), glyph-permutation null free and within frequency bins (2,000x), self-pairs excluded | Within words (raw2/raw3): Turkish +0.06 / -0.12, Hungarian +0.03 / -0.02, Finnish -0.04 / +0.01 (harmony languages NOT detected: consonant pairs swamp the vowel tier); Voynich ZL -0.01 / -0.18. Junction: Finnish +0.29 (p 0.001), Turkish -0.16, Hungarian -0.33, Latin -0.15, Hangul -0.08; Voynich ZL +0.21 (p 0.04), IT +0.20 (p 0.04), A +0.17, B +0.23; copy5 -0.12, copy8 -0.03 | The feature-free within-word test FAILS its positive control and is void. At junctions the Voynich leans weakly to 'similar glyphs attract' (p 0.04 in each transcription, 3 relations tested, so not significant after correction). No support for featural junction agreement |
| v29:V-29.3.4 | What the junction effect actually is: obs/expected for single glyph pairs (last glyph -> next first glyph, within-line shuffle x20), ZL and IT; [y q g] junction phi with words < 3 glyphs removed | y.q- 1.57x (3,418 vs 2,177), but y.y- 0.59x, g.q- 0.17x; n.q- 0.55x, l.q- 0.57x; n.o- 1.35x, n.ch- 1.45x; spacing fragments r.a- 2.6x, s.a- 3.7x, o.r- 4.2x. Identical in IT. [y q g] phi 0.236 -> 0.238 without short words | The top 'stroke feature' at junctions is a PAIRING, not agreement: final y calls initial q, final n (aiin) calls o/ch, and the same glyph across the junction (y.y) is AVOIDED. It is not carried by short fragments. Complementary pairing of word classes (y-words before q-words, n-words before o/ch-words), Grade B (replicates the known junction dependence of SUMMARY 'Holds' and v5 neighbour assimilation in a new form) |
| v29:V-29.3.S | Cycle summary | - | The Voynich has no featural spread: no stroke feature agrees within words (cycles 1-2) and the junction dependence is a glyph pairing with same-glyph avoidance, without sandhi-like adaptation, not produced by copy-and-edit, and not more than weakly linked to stroke similarity. The v25 featural design does not show up as phonological agreement |
| v26:V-26.1.1 | Massive random guessing: 96 random genomes (each gene on with p 0.15/0.3/0.5, values uniform), forged on the 103 fitted pages, fresh ridge AUC. Control: the all-off base genome (whole-book last-unit junction = v21 F1) drawn by chance 4 times | AUC percentiles 0/10/50/90/100: 0.926 / 0.946 / 0.998 / 1.000 / 1.000. Base genome 0.927-0.946 (4 draws). Best random: redup 0.02 + m/g expert 0.926; pos + chain + alliteration + drift 0.929. Switching ANY gene on at a random value raises mean AUC (+0.001 width to +0.016 ch/sh expert) | Random mechanisms make the forgery worse, never better: the landscape is a needle, not a plateau. Random guessing alone does not move below the plain junction rule (0.93) |
| v26:V-26.1.2 | First adversarial run (restart 0): random 96 -> (mu 12 + 16 offspring) x 12 generations, 432 evaluations; archive of 2 critics per generation. Bug found and fixed at gen 4: averaging fresh and archive AUC let a genome with fresh AUC 1.000 win by fooling stale critics (archive 0.554); fitness is now the WORSE of the two | Best fresh AUC (running mean of up to 4 seeds) by generation: 0.938, (1.000 bug), 0.933, 0.922, 0.919, 0.893, 0.874, 0.826, 0.853, 0.821, 0.787, 0.841; archive AUC 0.74-0.84. Winner at gen 12: junc=2 (rich key) + width filling + paragraph drift b_dr 3.64 (10.6 bits), seeds 0.821/0.844/0.859 | The evolution finds a path the v21 ladder never tried: line-to-line persistence of q-/a-/l- word make-up (drift) on top of the rich junction and width filling. Fresh AUC 0.93 -> ~0.84 (in-sample, selection-biased; re-tested with fresh seeds in cycle 3). Still far above the re-forge floor 0.54-0.68 |
| v26:V-26.2.1 | PLANTED: 64 random + 12 x (12 + 16) evolution on PL, 400 evaluations | Random best 0.971 (junc=0 + ch/sh expert); evolution 0.974 -> 0.946 (running mean). Winner sec + width + page urn 0.06 + b_cs 1.16; minimal genome by backward elimination: page urn 0.06 + b_cs 1.16 (3-4 seed AUC 0.951). Recovered: the ch/sh state with the right sign and size (1.16 vs 1.5) and a page-level copy pool (planted: a 3-line copy window). Missed: drift +4.0, m/g -1.0, sec/pos tables | Partial recovery: the strongest planted mechanism is found with the right sign; two of five are not |
| v26:V-26.2.2 | PLANTED calibration: the TRUE planted genome, refitted on its own 103 output pages, forges them; 5 seeds ridge/boosting; null (genome vs itself) and negative (genome re-forging its own output) | TRUE genome 0.956 / 0.957 (null 0.56 / 0.54, negative 0.90 / 0.89); evolved winner 0.954 / 0.952 (negative 0.86 / 0.91); base junction 0.994 / 0.992 (negative 0.60 / 0.57). True genome caught by C_rate z -15 (refitted tables already hold the ch/sh runs; the expert doubles them) | KEY CALIBRATION: even the right answer is caught at 0.956, because a refit-then-regenerate scribe with copying or state experts is not idempotent (it compounds its own effects). The evolution matched the true genome's score (0.954 vs 0.956), so search was not the bottleneck; the reachable floor for such genomes is their own negative control (0.86-0.90), not 0.54-0.68. Margins must be read as AUC minus the genome's own negative AUC |
| v26:V-26.2.3 | PLANTED held-out: tables from the fitted half forge the other half's skeletons | All generators 0.999-1.000 (winner, minimal, base, word bigram) | Held-out page AUC is useless here: real held-out pages carry words unseen in the fitted half (corpus_hapax, logfreq), forgeries cannot. Held-out tests use perplexity and survivor statistics instead (cycle 3) |
