| v26:V-26.4.3 | KILL TEST for the margin: the planted genome (copy + ch/sh + drift + m/g + sec/pos) applied to the Voynich and to Latin, where it is plainly wrong | V: AUC 1.000 / 0.997, negative 0.94 / 0.92, margin 0.06 / 0.08. LA: 1.000 / 0.996, negative 0.98 / 0.96, margin 0.02 / 0.04 | The margin is KILLED as a stand-alone metric: a wrong genome that compounds its own effects gets a margin as small as the Voynich winner's while being caught at AUC 1.0. Only the absolute AUC (and the floor of an idempotent re-forge, 0.52-0.60) can be trusted |
| v26:V-26.4.4 | Verdict on the requested contrast, absolute AUC with fresh seeds | Voynich best 0.796 / 0.843 (floor of idempotent base re-forge 0.52; v21 floor 0.54-0.68). Latin best 0.842 / 0.855 (floor 0.52-0.60). Planted text, true genome 0.956 | The Voynich does NOT reach the floor, and the herbal does NOT stall clearly above the Voynich (difference 0.01-0.05, within seed spread 0.72-0.85). The planted control shows the scribe family cannot even re-generate its own output (0.956), so 'generable' cannot be certified with refit-then-regenerate generators. Grade A for the negative statement (no genome reached the floor); the Voynich-vs-herbal contrast is NOT shown |
| v26:V-26.4.5 | REPLICATION: independent Voynich restart 1 (different random 64, 10 generations, 344 evaluations) | Random phase already best with drift (junc=2 + pfl + b_dr 6.88: 0.884); detours through sec + width + m/g quota + drift (0.86); final winner junc=2 + width + chain 0.23 + drift 7.19 (running mean 0.846-0.871) | The same four mechanisms as restart 0 (rich key, width, chain, drift), reached independently. Grade B: paragraph drift of q-/a-/l- word make-up from the line above is the single mechanism the v21 ladder lacked |

| v30:V-30.1 | DIALECT LADDER: cheapest context-sensitive rewrite (<= 30 ordered rules, one-pass transducer) turning Currier A into B, found by stochastic greedy search and scored on held-out pages (JSD of char 2/3-grams + words). Ladder from real 1350-1450 pairs run through the same pipeline: Commedia copies by different scribes (CATMuS), Bavarian vs Alemannic and Ripuarian (ReF), Latin vs Italian (CATMuS), Czech edition vs pre-Hus digraph spelling, German/Latin/Czech/Italian cross pairs, 3-letter-swap and full-substitution keys. Nulls: page-split A/A, B/B, ZL/IT, Markov resyntheses | Raw excess: nulls 0.00-0.04; B hand 2/3 0.02; Commedia 0.26; Bav->Alem 0.45; A->B 0.46 (herbal 0.48); same dialect other texts 0.58; Latin->Italian 0.78; reform 0.94; 3-swap key 1.23; unrelated 1.3-1.6; substitution 1.94. Nulls never gain on held-out data | A->B sits on the DIALECT rung, far below language or cipher-key change. Kills (A/A ~ A/B; A->B > Latin->Italian) not fired. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.2 | Planted 4-rule rewrite of A (positive); out-of-sample transfer (herbal-learned rules applied to unseen A pharma vs unseen B bio/stars/hand 3) against 300 random and 150 shuffled-rhs rule sets, calibrated with German and Latin transfers; 22,000 random 6-rule sets (share improving fit, survivors re-tested) | Plant closed 0.44-0.83, 1-2 of 4 rules exact. Transfer: -8% / -4% / -5% distance (German dialect -10%, to another dialect +1.5%, reform -53%); all random sets and 146/150 shuffled sets worse. Random sets improve A->B 1% (dialect 1%, same-language 0-1%, language/reform/cipher/unrelated 13-54%) | A->B rewrite is real and section-independent, and dialect-sized on a second, search-free ruler. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.3 | Guided search (60% proposals from over/under-represented substrings), 2 seeds; reversed directions; edge-only vs context-free rule families | Closure@30 A->B 0.47/0.48, herbal 0.60/0.67, B->A 0.45 vs dialect 0.27-0.32, Commedia 0.17-0.25, Latin->Italian 0.28-0.40, reform 0.56-0.69. Top rules 4/4 runs: A -chy/-chol/-chor/-or -> B -chedy/-chdy/-edy; word-initial cth reduced. Edge/free 0.38/0.38 (reform 0.41/0.86, cipher 0.37/0.75, dialect 0.20/0.18). Direction ratio 1.07 (dialect 1.08, reform 1.19) | A->B = dialect-sized but reform-regular, ending-centred, symmetric shift; not a letter key. Grade B for the ending family, C for symmetry. Not cracked (nothing outside the manuscript predicted) |
| v30:V-30.1.0 | NULLS: A split vs A split, B vs B, Latin vs Latin, Bavarian vs Bavarian (page halves of one corpus); A vs a trigram Markov resynthesis of A, same for B; ZL vs IT transliteration (disjoint A pages) | ex0: A/A +0.005, B/B -0.007, A herbal/A herbal +0.010, ZL/IT -0.009, Markov A +0.039, Markov B +0.031; held closure always <= 0 (rewrites learned on fit make held worse, by 0.003-0.02) | Nulls pass: same-source pairs sit on the floor and the search cannot buy held-out gain there. The rewrite gains below are not search artefacts |
| v30:V-30.1.1 | Within-B scribes: B hand 2 (mostly biological) -> B hand 3 (stars, herbal), 2 seeds | ex0 0.023 (A/A null 0.005, Markov 0.03-0.04); closure -0.06 | Hands 2 and 3 of Currier B write statistically the same text: distance at null level, across different sections. B is one homogeneous system |
| v30:V-30.1.2 | LADDER of raw distance ex0 (mean of 2 seeds, 4,500 words per side) | same work other scribes (Commedia) 0.26; dialect Bavarian->Alemannic 0.45; **Voynich A->B 0.46 (B->A 0.49; herbal A->herbal B 0.48 at small budget, where Bav->Alem is 0.42 and Commedia 0.28)**; same dialect other texts 0.58; far dialect Bav->Ripuarian 0.71; Latin->Italian 0.78; spelling reform 0.94; 3-letter-swap cipher 1.23; unrelated languages 1.29-1.62; full substitution 1.94 | A->B falls on the DIALECT rung: above two scribes copying the same work, level with Bavarian vs Alemannic, below everything language-level and far below any cipher key change (even swapping 3 letters costs 2.7x the A/B distance) |
| v30:V-30.1.3 | Shape of the cheapest rewrite: held closure after 10 and 30 rules, share of held gain carried by the top 3 rules (top3) | closure@30: A->B 0.48 (seeds 0.60 / 0.37), B->A 0.40, herbal 0.32; dialect 0.26; Commedia 0.13-0.16; Latin->Italian 0.35; reform 0.52; unrelated 0.37-0.47; ciphers 0.44 (search does not finish a 25-letter key in 30 rules). top3: A->B 0.56 (seed 0: 0.76), reform 0.40, dialects 0.29-0.35, languages 0.23-0.30 | A->B is the most CONCENTRATED rewrite on the ladder. Seed 0: two rules, chy -> edy at word end (+0.060) and or -> edy anywhere (+0.110), give 0.17 of the 0.23 held gain. The cheapest A->B move is to turn A's -y / -or endings into B's -edy. On herbal-only text the concentration falls to 0.38 (dialect-like) |
| v30:V-30.1.4 | Kill tests for the brief: (i) split A vs split A costs as much as A vs B? (ii) A->B costs more than Latin->Italian? | (i) 0.005 vs 0.46: no. (ii) 0.46 vs 0.78: no | Neither kill fires. A/B is a real but sub-language distance |
| v30:V-30.2.1 | POSITIVE planted control on the Voynich alphabet: A page-half X vs other A page-half Y rewritten by 4 planted rules (o->a word-initial, dy->dey word-final, k->t, aiin->ain); cycle-1 search, 15 rules, 2 seeds | Planted excess 0.53-0.57 (larger than A->B 0.46). Held closure 0.64 / 0.44; exact planted rules recovered 1/4 and 0/4 (k->t never proposed: the random proposal hits one given single-glyph rule ~1% per step) | The cycle-1 search measures DISTANCE and closure reliably (nulls flat, plant closed by half) but is too weak to recover exact rules. Rule lists in cycle 1 are therefore frequency trades, not a reconstructed rulebook; the 'chy/or -> edy' reading stays grade C |
| v30:V-30.2.2 | OUT-OF-SAMPLE TRANSFER: 20 rules learned on ALL herbal A -> ALL herbal B, applied unchanged to A pharma (never seen) and scored against B biological, B stars, B hand 3 (never seen). Controls: 100 random rule sets of the same size from the same proposal pool; 50 sets with the learned lhs and shuffled rhs. Calibration: Bav1->Alemannic rules applied to other Bavarian texts (Bav2) vs Alemannic and vs Ripuarian; Latin->Italian prose rules applied to Latin vs the Commedia; Czech reform rules applied to the other half of Dalimil | Voynich pharma->bio 1.000 -> 0.920 (-8%), ->stars 0.813 -> 0.781 (-4%), ->hand 3 0.826 -> 0.782 (-5%); random sets median +0.41..+0.48, best random always worse than d0; shuffled rhs -0.5%..+4%. German Bav2->Alem -10%, Bav2->Ripuarian +1.5% (rules do not carry to another dialect); Latin->Commedia -13%; Czech reform -53% | PASSES: the A->B rewrite learned on herbal pages predicts B's statistics in sections it never saw, beating all 300 random rule sets and 146 of 150 shuffled-rhs sets, with the same size of effect as a real dialect rewrite (-4..-8% vs -10%) and far below a spelling reform (-53%). The learned herbal rules again build B's -edy/-ed endings (eor->dy, cho->edy word-final, ees->edy, hai->hed, har->hdy word-final). Grade B: the A/B difference is one systematic, section-independent shift of dialect size |
| v30:V-30.2.3 | MASSIVE RANDOM GUESSING: 2,000 random 6-rule sets per pair (22,000 sets); share that improves the fit distance; top 1% by fit re-scored on held-out pages (survivors) vs 40 random sets | Share improving fit: A->B 0.01; A/A, B/B, B2/B3 null 0.00; Commedia 0.00; Bav->Alem 0.01; same dialect other texts 0.01; Latin->Italian 0.13; reform 0.17; 3-swap cipher 0.28; German->Italian 0.54. Survivors' held gain: A->B +0.008 (70% positive), Bav->Alem -0.001 (50%), Bav/Bav +0.003 (70%), Commedia -0.003 (20%), nulls -0.003..-0.007 (5%), Latin->Italian +0.031, reform +0.072, cipher +0.063, unrelated +0.072 (100%) | A second, search-free ruler gives the same rung: random rewrites almost never help A->B, exactly as for a dialect or same-language pair, while they often help between languages, across a spelling reform or a key change. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.3.0 | POSITIVE: planted 4-rule rewrite of A (as V-30.2.1), guided search, 15 rules, 2 seeds | Held closure 0.83 / 0.48 (unguided 0.64 / 0.44); exact rules recovered 2/4 and 1/4, plus near-equivalents (dy->dey without context; k->'' and dy->'' partial) | Guided search recovers most of a planted Voynich-alphabet rewrite; good enough to compare rule SHAPES, still not a guaranteed rulebook |
| v30:V-30.3.1 | Guided ladder, 2 seeds, 30 rules, same splits as cycle 1; nulls rerun | Nulls: A/A, B/B, B2/B3 held closure <= 0.10, excess +-0.02. closure@30 (seed 0 / seed 1): **A->B 0.47 / 0.48; herbal A->herbal B 0.60 / 0.67; B->A 0.47 / 0.43**; dialect Bav->Alem 0.30 / 0.29 (small budget 0.32 / 0.29; reversed 0.27); same dialect other texts 0.35; Commedia 0.25 / 0.17 (reversed 0.20); Latin->Italian 0.40 / 0.39 (reversed 0.28); spelling reform 0.69 / 0.64 (reversed 0.56); 3-swap cipher 0.67; full substitution 0.56; German->Italian 0.51 | Seeds now agree. At the SAME raw distance as a dialect pair (ex0 0.46-0.48 vs 0.42-0.49), the A->B gap closes 1.6-2.2x as far as any dialect or same-language pair, close to a spelling reform. A->B = dialect-sized distance with reform-like regularity |
| v30:V-30.3.2 | Which rules carry the gain (held-out marginal gain, guided runs, 4 runs) | Every run's top rules build B's -dy / -edy endings out of A's -y/-or/-ol endings: A->B chy->edy (+0.060, +0.047), eol->edy (+0.040), or->edy word-final (+0.058), o->dy word-final (+0.028); herbal hol->hdy word-final (+0.061), chy->edy word-final (+0.039), hor->hdy (+0.051), hor->edy word-final (+0.033); and word-initial cth deleted or turned into ch/lch (+0.016..+0.039 in 4/4) | Grade B (reproduced in 4/4 guided runs, 2 unguided runs, and the cycle-2 transfer rules): the cheapest A->B rewrite is (1) A's -chy/-chor/-chol endings -> B's -chedy/-chdy, (2) fewer word-initial cth. Two clusters carry about half the gain; the rest is scattered frequency trading |
| v30:V-30.3.3 | Direction asymmetry X->Y vs Y->X (closure@30, guided, mean of 2 seeds) | A->B 0.48 vs B->A 0.45 (ratio 1.07); spelling reform 0.67 vs reversed 0.56 (1.19, many-to-one: diacritics are lost); dialect 0.30 vs 0.27 (1.08); Latin->Italian 0.40 vs 0.28 (1.40); Commedia 0.21 vs 0.20 | A/B is nearly symmetric, like a dialect pair; no sign that one side was derived from the other by a lossy many-to-one reform. Grade C (one pair per rung; would kill: an asymmetry > 1.2 on IT2a) |
| v30:V-30.3.4 | WHERE the rewrite lives: same guided search restricted to word-edge rules only vs context-free rules only (20 rules, seed 0) | edge / free closure: A->B 0.38 / 0.38; B->A 0.30 / 0.39; spelling reform 0.41 / 0.86; 3-swap cipher 0.37 / 0.75; dialect 0.20 / 0.18; Latin->Italian 0.25 / 0.35; Commedia 0.17 / 0.08; same dialect 0.30 / 0.28 | Letter-level systems (reform, key change) are closed by context-free rules at twice the edge rate; scribal habits (Commedia) live at word edges; dialects are even. A->B is EVEN, like a dialect, but at twice a dialect's level: the difference sits in glyph clusters (chy/chol/chor vs chedy), at the end of words and inside them, not in a letter-for-letter key. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.F1 | Where does the A->B distance fall? | Raw excess (mean of 2 seeds): nulls 0.00-0.04; B hand 2 vs B hand 3 0.02; same work other scribes 0.26; dialect (Bavarian->Alemannic) 0.45; **A->B 0.46 (herbal only 0.48)**; same dialect other texts 0.58; far dialect 0.71; Latin->Italian 0.78; spelling reform 0.94; 3-letter-swap key 1.23; unrelated 1.29-1.62; full substitution 1.94. Random 6-rule sets improve A->B 1% of the time, as for dialect and same-language pairs (0-1%), vs 13-54% for language, reform, cipher and unrelated pairs | DIALECT RUNG. Grade B (one corpus per rung; content not matched). Kills not fired: split A vs split A is ~0, and A->B < Latin->Italian |
| v30:V-30.F2 | What do the rules look like? | Guided search closes 0.47-0.48 of the A->B gap (herbal 0.60-0.67) vs 0.27-0.35 for dialect and same-language pairs, 0.17-0.25 for Commedia copies, 0.64-0.69 for a spelling reform. Top rules in 4/4 runs: A -chy/-chol/-chor/-or -> B -chedy/-chdy/-edy, and word-initial cth reduced. Edge-only and context-free rules close A->B equally (0.38/0.38), unlike reform/cipher (free 2x edge) | SYSTEMATIC, ENDING-CENTRED, NOT A LETTER KEY. A->B is a dialect-sized but reform-regular shift, dominated by one ending family. Grade B |
| v30:V-30.F3 | Does it predict outside the fitting data? | Rules learned on herbal A->herbal B move unseen A pharma toward unseen B biological (-8%), stars (-4%), hand 3 (-5%); all 300 random rule sets and 146/150 shuffled-rhs sets do worse; German dialect rules transfer the same way (-10%) but not to another dialect (+1.5%) | YES, out-of-sample within the manuscript (grade B). Nothing outside the manuscript is predicted, so nothing is read: NOT cracked |
| v30:V-30.F4 | Within B | B hand 2 vs hand 3 (different sections) sits at null level (0.02) | Grade A (in this metric): B's two main scribes share one system; the A/B split is not a scribe-by-scribe drift |
| v30:V-30.F5 | Direction | A->B 0.48 vs B->A 0.45 (dialect 1.08, reform 1.19, Latin/Italian 1.40) | Nearly symmetric; no sign of a lossy one-way reform. Grade C |
| v30:V-30.F | Overall | - | Currier A and B are two conventions of ONE system at dialect distance, separated mainly by a regular ending change (A -y/-or/-ol vs B -edy/-dy) plus fewer word-initial cth in B. Not two languages, not two cipher keys, not two scribes' noise. Next: run the ladder on IT2a and on a second dialect pair (ReF Swabian/Bavarian) to firm grade B; test whether the -edy rule is line-position or paragraph-position conditioned |

ENHG allo +0.10/+0.12/+0.09, plain null. CATMuS Latin with abbreviation marks +0.25 / -0.11 / +0.14 (ppmi p 0.0002), plain +0.10/+0.01/+0.07. Many p < 0.01 | YES, PARTLY: graphic-unit transcriptions of an arbitrary alphabet DO give a significant positive shape-behaviour r (typically +0.10 to +0.20, best +0.25 on one model), so v25's printed-font null (~0) was too easy a baseline. The non-featural source is mostly the combining/abbreviation marks and minim strokes (small, similar-looking units that all attach after letters), not the allographs |

| v28:V-28.2.V1 | Verdict: other scripts (mean image-combo r over fonts, ppmi / svd / potts) | Ethiopic Amharic (abugida, 208 syllables) +0.17 / +0.08 / +0.06 (p 0.0002), featural layout decomposition +0.16 / +0.13 / +0.25 (p 0.0002); Tigrinya replication image ppmi +0.03 to +0.06, potts +0.05 to +0.07 (p 0.0002-0.09), layout +0.18 / potts +0.25. OCS Cyrillic +0.13 / +0.07 / +0.03 (ppmi p 0.003-0.01); the same text in Glagolitic -0.00 / -0.00 / +0.02 (null); Russian +0.01 / +0.02 / +0.00; Armenian +0.04 / +0.05 / +0.06 (n.s.) | Positive control behaves: the near-featural abugida shows the link, the arbitrary alphabets do not (Glagolitic null on identical behaviour shows shape, not text, carries it; OCS Cyrillic's weak link sits in its composite iotated letters). The Voynich image link exceeds even Ethiopic's on all three models (and its hand link exceeds Ethiopic's layout-featural link on ppmi and potts). Grade A |
| v28:V-28.2.V2 | Verdict: does the EVA segmentation create the effect? | Every EVA character a unit (benches split into c + h): ZL +0.37 / +0.34 / +0.38, IT +0.39 / +0.36 / +0.41. i-runs and e-runs as units: ZL +0.27 / +0.28 / +0.37, IT +0.27 / +0.31 / +0.39. Benched gallows split into ch + gallows: +0.24 / +0.37 / +0.48. Split + 6 BPE chunk merges: +0.22 / +0.34 / +0.48. All p <= 0.003, partial given position +0.20 to +0.48 | NO. The link survives every segmentation tried, merged or split, in both transcriptions; splitting the benches makes ppmi stronger. Grade A |
| v28:V-28.2.V3 | Verdict: can composite (multi-letter) units fake it? | Printed Latin with 10 BPE merges +0.05 / +0.05 / +0.08 (p >= 0.02); 25 merges +0.09 / +0.10 / +0.17 (potts p 0.0002), but at 23 units (V-28.3) ~0 | Composite units sharing letters create a weak link that grows with the number of composites, at most 40% of the Voynich potts value; the Voynich link is strongest when no composites exist (split). Grade B |
| v28:V-28.2.V4 | 15th-16th-c. cipher alphabets / invented scripts | Searched DECODE, HF, Zenodo, web: no openly downloadable transcribed corpus of a 15th-16th-c. cipher or invented script with glyph images found (DECODE needs a login; Rohonc Codex has no public machine transcription found; Copiale is 18th c., transcribed as symbol names with line images only, no per-symbol images) | NOT RUN. Open gap: the strongest untested rival class. Would need a human-made per-symbol image set (e.g. Copiale symbol crops) |

| v28:V-28.3.PL1 | PLANTED positional allographs in printed Latin: the word-final form of the 6 commonest letters made a separate unit, drawn IDENTICALLY (latinprint+pallo6) or in the italic cut (pallo6i); full v25 pipeline | identical: r -0.04 to -0.01 (ppmi/potts, 3 fonts, all p > 0.5); italic: -0.01 to +0.09 (p >= 0.08). The allograph-likeness detector ranks exactly the 6 planted pairs on top (i/i', e/e', s/s', t/t', u/u', a/a') | Positional variants CANNOT make the v25 link: under v25's behaviour (L1 R1 L2 R2 with word edges) a final form and its medial twin have different contexts by construction, so look-alike positional variants add nothing. Detector calibrated (finds the planted pairs) |
| v28:V-28.3.V1 | Verdict: size-matched (23 most frequent units each) | Voynich ZL +0.28 / +0.30 / +0.43, IT +0.29 / +0.26 / +0.44, split +0.37 / +0.34 / +0.38 (all p <= 0.001). Rivals: printed Latin -0.04 / +0.02 / -0.05; Latin BPE25 ~0; CATMuS +0.12 / -0.14 / +0.06 (n.s.); Castilian allographetic -0.02 to +0.17 (mostly n.s.); ENHG +0.04 / 0.00 / +0.03; Ethiopic +0.13 / +0.10 / +0.07 (n.s.); OCS +0.13 / 0.00 / +0.01; Glagolitic, Russian, Armenian ~0. Only rival reaching significance on two models: Castilian Val_S hand C MINIM-split +0.26 / +0.13 / +0.26 (p 0.001-0.04) and hand D minim potts +0.23 (p 0.003) | At equal inventory size the graphic-unit rivals mostly fall to noise; the closest is a minim-stroke segmentation of a Gothic hand, which matches the Voynich on ppmi but reaches 60% of its potts value and half its svd. v25 SURVIVES. Grade A |
| v28:V-28.3.V2 | Verdict: positional variants | Voynich: r is HIGHER among pairs that share word positions (ZL ppmi +0.30, potts +0.50; IT +0.28 / +0.51) than among complementary pairs (+0.23 / +0.35); dropping the 3-10 most allograph-like pairs leaves r unchanged (+0.28-0.30 / +0.43-0.44). Medieval sets: removing the 9-20 KNOWN allograph pairs changes r by <= 0.02 | KILLED as an explanation (both by the planted control and on the data): the Voynich link is not carried by positional variants, and real allographs do not carry the link in medieval hands either. | v34:V-34.1 | Cycle verdict | - | Voynich line-final glyphs do not respond to physical crowding (grade B negative), but the Latin positive control does not respond either, so the test cannot tell 'no abbreviation' from 'abbreviation not driven by room'. Kill rule of idea #1 needs the Latin slope; it is absent |
| v34:V-34.2.1 | POSITIVE CONTROL, same image pipeline: tightness vs last-token abbreviation | abbr_last z -0.65, abbr_last_any -0.30, with length covariate -0.67; halves A +1.15, B -1.39; tight lines 0.445 marks vs roomy 0.525 | Still no abbreviation response in Latin. | v34:V-34.2.2 | What Latin DOES do in tight lines | dens_rest z +6.4 (halves +2.7, +6.1): tight lines are written with wider spacing all along; last word slightly squeezed (comp_last z -2.4) | Latin scribes fill the line by spreading the whole line (justification), not by abbreviating the last word |
| v34:V-34.2.3 | Same features in the Voynich | dens_rest z +0.4 (recto +1.6, verso -1.0); comp_last z +3.8 (recto +3.5, verso +1.4, opposite sign to Latin) | The Latin line-spreading signature is ABSENT: Voynich line density does not track how close the line comes to the margin. Line reach is set by what is written (words per line z +4.4), not by stretching. comp_last does not replicate across halves (artefact-prone, grade C at best) |
| v34:V-34.2.4 | Final glyphs m, g, n, y and last-word length, recto vs verso | all |z| < 1 in verso; recto nglyph_last z -3.1 (pFW 0.01), verso +0.8 | The only half-significant effect (shorter last words in tight recto lines) does not replicate. No glyph behaves like an abbreviation mark under crowding |
| v34:V-34.2 | Cycle verdict | - | Same pipeline, same null: Latin shows a strong layout response (spread spacing) but no abbreviation response; the Voynich shows neither. Grade C: 'later in the line = more a, fewer e'. Would support: same drift in IT2a and on herbal pages with drawings; would kill: it vanishes once word length is matched |
| v34:V-34.3 | Cycle verdict | - | The null on m/g/-am is a powered null: crowding-driven final-m abbreviation in >= 1 in 5 tight lines is excluded (grade B). No Voynich glyph behaves as an abbreviation mark under crowding. But the Latin control never abbreviates more under crowding either, so 'abbreviation' as such is not excluded, only room-driven abbreviation |
| v34:V-34.F1 | Do Voynich line-final glyphs respond to crowding? | 27 features (final m g n y l r s d o, -am -aiin -ain -dy -ey -ol -ar -al -or, length, frequency, hapax, uncertain spaces, gallows): none survives familywise (best |z| 2.1); final m z -0.4, g +0.5, -am -1.1. Planted m-abbreviation in 20% of tight lines is found 75%, at 35% always | NO (grade B negative, powered): m, g and -am are line-end markers independent of the room left. Crowding-driven m-abbreviation in more than ~1 of 5 tight lines is excluded |

Latin scribes here do not abbreviate more when the line is crowded; idea #1's kill rule ('Latin shows the slope') cannot be applied. The abbreviation question stays open in principle |
| v34:V-34.F3 | How do the two scripts fill a line? | Latin: tight lines are spread wider all along (density z +6.4, both halves); last word slightly squeezed (z -2.4). Voynich: density flat (z +0.4; recto +1.6, verso -1.0); reach set by words per line (z +4.4) | Grade B: the Voynich scribe does NOT justify lines by spacing, unlike the Latin control. Lines are composed to length, consistent with v26 line-width filling being content-side |
| v34:V-34.F4 | Greedy wrapping? | next-line first-word length vs tightness: print z -15.1, Latin MSS -2.2, Voynich +0.8 | Grade A (this sample): Voynich lines are not running text wrapped at the margin (physical confirmation of the sealed line) |
| v34:V-34.F5 | Candidate abbreviation glyphs | none respond to crowding. Side result: a left-to-right drift inside lines (a, r, p up; e, k, sh, q down; 19-24/25 pages) that does not grow with crowding | Grade C side finding (would support: same in IT2a and other sections; would kill: disappears with word length matched) |
| v34:V-34.F | Overall | - | Voynich line endings do not respond to physical crowding; no glyph behaves like a crowding-driven abbreviation mark (powered null for m). The Latin control does not abbreviate under crowding either, so this kills 'room-driven abbreviation', not abbreviation. The strongest new contrast is layout: Latin justifies by spacing, the Voynich does not. Not cracked |

Max over phase and post-gap offset of |corr(feature, position in cycle)|, P 2-40, 5 features (opening word length, gallows in it, first line length, words, edit distance of the opening word to the previous entry's); 100 shuffles (the edit distance recomputed in shuffled order) | Real calendar (Ado, date only): zmax 11.6 at P30 (edit distance to previous opening word), also gallows-analogue 8.5 and opening length 7.2 at P30, p 0.010 (floor). Ado text only: 3.6, p 0.26. VOYNICH star units: zmax 3.6 (first line length P31), p 0.29; IT2a 3.6 (words P17), p 0.18; biological B 3.0, p 0.62 | No day counter in the entry openings. Grade A negative (a Roman-numeral-like count is caught at z 11.6) |
| v32:V-32.3.2 | Markov null: 20 word-bigram resyntheses of the star units (unit and line lengths kept, opening words drawn from real openings) scored on the real data's shuffle-null moments | Resyntheses zmax 4.3-9.2 (median 6.0); real Voynich 4.9, p 0.86 | The real sequence is no more periodic than a period-free resynthesis |
| v32:V-32.3.3 | Dose-response: planted calendars in the real 339 star units, 60 shuffles each. Opening word replaced by the day's word in s of entries; s of body words from a 5-word day list; length scaled by a day factor in 1 +- s | Opening, P7: s 0.1 z 7.9 p 0.066, s 0.2 z 23 p 0.016, s 0.3 z 32 p 0.016. Opening, P29.53: s 0.1 z 5.1 p 0.66, s 0.2 z 17 p 0.016, s 0.3 z 27 p 0.016. Day vocabulary 1-4 %: not found (p 0.54-0.80). Length +-10-20 %: not found (p 0.64-0.89) | Reach: a calendar marked by a day word in >= 20 % of entry openings would have been seen at both 7 and 29.5; a day mark in ~10 % at period 7 is borderline; calendars carried only by sparse body vocabulary or by entry length are invisible at this size. The Voynich negative is an upper bound only for written, opening-position dating |
| v32:V-32.3.V | Verdict, cycle 3 | - | No sawtooth counter, no excess over a Markov resynthesis, and the reach is now measured: openings with day marks in >= 20 % of entries are excluded (Grade A); a calendar order carried only in sparse content or unwritten is untested (Grade C, open) |
